\displaystyle=\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\partial_{\bm{v}}\left(\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}\|_{L^{2}}\right)_{\bm{v}={\widetilde{\bm{v}}}}=\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\partial_{\bm{u}}\left(\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}\|_{L^{2}}\right)_{\bm{u}={\widetilde{\bm{u}}}}=\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}}.

The KKT conditions (
34
) thus simplify to

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{h}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\lambda_{v}\bm{\xi}_{g}

\displaystyle=(\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v})({\widetilde{\bm{v}}};\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}),

(35a)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{h}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\lambda_{u}\bm{\xi}_{h}

\displaystyle=(\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u})({\widetilde{\bm{u}}};\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}),

(35b)

where we have substituted for the operators
\mathsf{g}
and
\mathsf{h}
from Eq. (
26
) and used
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}=\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}=0
.

Now note that the random vectors
\bm{\xi}_{g}
and
\bm{\xi}_{h}
are chosen independently of the collections
\{\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
and
\{\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
, respectively.
In Eqs. (
35
) defining the pair
({\widetilde{\bm{u}}},{\widetilde{\bm{v}}})
, suppose for the sake of contradiction that
\lambda_{v}>0
. Then
{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}
depends nontrivially on
\bm{\xi}_{g}
. On the other hand,
\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}=0
, so the random vector
\widehat{\bm{v}}{}
is a measurable function of the collection
\{\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
, which is a contradiction. Thus, we must have
\lambda_{v}=0
. Reasoning via a parallel argument, we also have
\lambda_{u}=0
.

To conclude, note that the KKT conditions (
35
) simplify to Eq. (
30
) when
\lambda_{v}=\lambda_{u}=0
and under the convention that
\bm{0}/\|\bm{0}\|_{L^{2}}=0
.

Case 2:
\min(\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}},\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}},\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}},\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}})>0
:

In this case, the subdifferentials in Eq. (
34
) are derivatives, and we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\bm{v}}\left(\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}\|_{L^{2}}\right)_{\bm{v}={\widetilde{\bm{v}}}}=\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\partial_{\bm{u}}\left(\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}\|_{L^{2}}\right)_{\bm{u}={\widetilde{\bm{u}}}}=\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}}

(36)

Furthermore, since
\min(\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}},\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}})>0
, we have
\lambda_{v}=\lambda_{u}=0
, since neither of the constraints is met with equality. Consequently, Eq. (
34
) simplifies to Eq. (
30
), as claimed.

Proof of Claim
1
:

We prove each subclaim in turn.

Establishing (i):

We will first show that if
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}=0
, then
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
. Since
\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}=0
and because
\bm{\xi}_{h}
is chosen independently of
\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}
,
we must have that
\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
.
The first KKT condition (
34
) can then be written as

\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{h}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\lambda_{v}\bm{\xi}_{g}=(\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v})({\widetilde{\bm{v}}};\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}),

(37)

and the second KKT condition (
34
) as

\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{h}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+(\sqrt{\Lambda}\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}+\lambda_{u})\bm{\xi}_{h}=(\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u})({\widetilde{\bm{u}}};\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})

(38)

Suppose that
\sqrt{\Lambda}\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}+\lambda_{u}>0
. Then by condition (
38
), the vector
{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}
depends nontrivially on
\bm{\xi}_{h}
, which is in turn independent of everything else. But
\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}=0
, and so
{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}
is a measurable function of
\{\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
and cannot depend on
\bm{\xi}_{h}
. This leads to a contradiction, and so
\sqrt{\Lambda}\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}+\lambda_{u}=0
. Since both terms are nonnegative, we must have
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}=\lambda_{u}=0
.

A parallel argument under the supposition that
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}=0
yields
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}=\lambda_{v}=0
. Putting these two pieces together, we see that
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}=0
if and only if
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}=0
, and if so then
\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
and
\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
.

Establishing (ii):

Expanding the definition of the operator
\mathsf{g}
from Eq. (
26
) and using the fact that
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}>0
,
the first KKT condition (
34
) can be written as

(\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}+\lambda_{v})\bm{\xi}_{g}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}}\cdot\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}+(\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v})({\widetilde{\bm{v}}};\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}).

(39)

In particular, viewing
{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}
as fixed, the vector
{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}
can be viewed as the minimizer—under the constraint that
\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,\bm{v}\rangle_{L^{2}}\geq 0
—of the function
\alpha(\bm{v})+\beta(v)-\gamma(\bm{v})
, where

\displaystyle\alpha(\bm{v})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}\|_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\beta(\bm{v})

\displaystyle=\phi_{t}^{v}(\bm{v};\bm{V}_{k})+\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\,\bm{v}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\,\bm{v}\rangle_{L^{2}},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\gamma(\bm{v})

\displaystyle=\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,\bm{v}\rangle_{L^{2}}.

We now show that if in addition
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}>0
, then
\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}>0
.
Suppose that
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}>0
.
In preparing to apply Lemma
C.2
from the appendix, set

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp},\quad x_{0}=\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{g},\quad c_{1}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad f=\beta,

(40)

and note that
f
is
L
-smooth. We also have that
x_{0}\neq 0
since
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}>0
, and that
c_{1}\geq 0
. Note that
x_{1}
in the notation of Lemma
C.2
is the solution to the unconstrained problem

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{v}}=\argmin_{\bm{v}}\;\alpha(\bm{v})+\beta(\bm{v}),

(41)

and is unique since the function
\alpha+\beta
is strongly convex. Finally, we have
Ax_{2}=\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\neq 0
. Applying
Lemma
C.2
then directly implies that
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}>0
, which in turn implies that
\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}>0
.

Executing the same argument with the second KKT condition (
34
), we obtain that
\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}>0
. Putting together the two pieces completes the proof.
∎

4.4.2
Proof of Proposition
4.2

Recall the objects constructed in Eq. (
31
).
By definition (
26
) of the operator
\mathsf{g}[\,\cdot\,]
and since
\bm{\xi}_{g}
is an independent standard Gaussian scaled by
d^{-1/2}
, we see that
\bm{g}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is jointly Gaussian with
\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}
and has independent entries.
Similarly using the definition of the operator
\mathsf{h}[\,\cdot\,]
and since
\bm{\xi}_{h}
is an independent standard Gaussian scaled by
n^{-1/2}
, the vector
\bm{h}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is jointly Gaussian with
\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}
and has independent entries.
Next, we have that
\widehat{\bm{u}}{}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\in\mathsf{span}\{\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}},\,\ell\leq k+1\}
and
\widehat{\bm{v}}{}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\in\mathsf{span}\{\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}},\,\ell\leq k+1\}
by construction. Moreover, since
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\mathsf{g}[{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}]
, combining Eqs. (
31c
) and (
30a
) yields

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}-\widehat{\bm{v}}{}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\mathsf{g}[{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}]-\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{h}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\|_{L^{2}}}=(\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v})(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1};\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}).

(42a)

Similarly, combining Eqs. (
31b
) and (
30b
) with the definition
\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\mathsf{h}[{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}]
yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}-\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\mathsf{h}[{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}]-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}}=(\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u})(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1};\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}).

(42b)

Taken together, the calculations in Eq. (
42
) verify Eq. (
24
). Furthermore, Eq. (
23
) is immediately true. To complete the induction step,
it remains to verify Eq. (
22
).

By the definition (
26
) of the operator
\mathsf{g}[\,\cdot\,]
, we have that for
\ell\leq k
,

\displaystyle\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\mathsf{g}[{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}]\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}\Bigl\langle\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell^{\prime}},\sum_{\ell^{\prime\prime}=1}^{k}\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}\bm{\xi}_{g}\Bigr\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{=}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\,{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}.

(43)

Here step
{\sf(i)}
follows from expanding
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}
as a linear combination of the orthonormal vectors
\{\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell^{\prime}}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}
, and step
{\sf(ii)}
uses the orthonormality of these vectors along with the fact that
\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
for all
\ell\leq k
.
Now by the induction hypothesis, we have
\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}\rangle_{L^{2}}
for all
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\leq k
. Putting together the pieces, we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\quad\text{ for all }\ell\leq k,

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from the orthonormality of the vectors
\{\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}
and the fact that
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}
is in the span of these vectors. At the same time, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{g}[{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}],\,\mathsf{g}[{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}]\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}\bm{\xi}_{g}\Bigr\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\rangle^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

(44)

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we have used the orthonormality of the collection
\{\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k},\bm{\xi}_{g}
.
Combining Eqs. (
43
) and (
44
) verifies the induction hypothesis Eq. (
22
)(a). Proceeding in an identical manner with
(\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})
instead of
(\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})
verifies the induction hypothesis Eq. (
22
)(b), since the collection
\{\bm{h}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k},\bm{\xi}_{h}
is also orthonormal.

To verify (
22
)(c), we substitute the definition of
\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}
from Eq. (
31b
) to see that for any
\ell\leq k
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}},\widehat{\bm{u}}{}_{t}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{v}_{t}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{v}_{t}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{=}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}},\bm{v}_{t}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}.

(45)

In step
{\sf(i)}
, we have used the induction hypothesis to write
\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}
for all
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\leq k
. On the other hand, step
{\sf(ii)}
follows from the orthonormality of the vectors
\{\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}\}_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{\ell}
and the fact that
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}
is in the span of these vectors.

At the same time, using the definition of
\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}
from Eq. (
31b
) and noting that
{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}=\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}
and
{\widetilde{\bm{v}}}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}
once again yields

\displaystyle\langle\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}},\widehat{\bm{u}}{}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\frac{\langle\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}},\,\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{k}\langle\bm{g}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp},\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathsf{g}[{\widetilde{\bm{u}}}],\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}.

(46)

Combining Eqs. (
45
) and (
4.4.2
) verifies Eq. (
22
)(c). Eq. (
22
)(d) can be similarly verified via a parallel argument on the term
\langle\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE}},\widehat{\bm{v}}{}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}
.
∎

4.4.3
Proof of Proposition
4.3

As hypothesized, any solution to the fixed-point equations must have
(\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})
jointly Gaussian as well as
(\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})
jointly Gaussian, with the entries of these drawn i.i.d. across the row dimension. Define the collections
\{\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
and
\{\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
as the orthonormal vectors from before, noting that each of these also has i.i.d. entries.
Owing to joint Gaussianity and the fact that
\bm{g}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}
must have i.i.d. entries, there must exist scalars
\{L_{k+1,\ell}^{g},L_{k+1,\ell}^{h}\}_{\ell=1}^{k+1}
such that
\bm{g}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}L^{g}_{k+1,\ell}\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+L^{g}_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\xi}_{g}
for an independent Gaussian
\bm{\xi}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{d}/d)
, and
similarly,
\bm{h}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}L^{h}_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+L^{h}_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\xi}_{h}
for
\bm{\xi}_{h}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{n}/n)
.

Any solution to the fixed point equations must also satisfy Eq. (
23
), in that we have
\widehat{\bm{v}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\in\mathsf{span}(\{\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1})
and
\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\in\mathsf{span}(\{\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1})
. Define the collection
\{\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
and
\{\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
as the orthonormal vectors from before—there must exist scalars
\{\zeta_{k+1,\ell}^{u},\zeta_{k+1,\ell}^{v}\}_{\ell=1}^{k+1}
such that
\widehat{\bm{u}}{}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\zeta_{k+1,\ell}^{u}\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\zeta_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}
and
\widehat{\bm{v}}{}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\zeta_{k+1,\ell}^{v}\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\zeta_{k+1,k+1}^{v}\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}
.
The SE equations must hold: Explicitly writing out Eqs. (
22
)(a) and (b), we must have that

\displaystyle L^{g}_{k+1,\ell}

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle L^{h}_{k+1,\ell}

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}\quad\text{ for all }\ell\leq k;

(47a)

\displaystyle L^{g}_{k+1,k+1}

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle^{1/2}_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle^{1/2}_{L^{2}}=\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle L^{h}_{k+1,k+1}

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle^{1/2}_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle^{1/2}_{L^{2}}=\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}.

(47b)

Similarly, writing out Eqs. (
22
)(c) and (d), we must have

\displaystyle\zeta_{k+1,\ell}^{u}=\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}\;\;\text{ and }\;

\displaystyle\zeta_{k+1,\ell}^{v}=\langle\widehat{\bm{v}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}\text{ for all }\ell\leq k;

(47c)

as well as

\displaystyle\zeta_{k+1,k+1}^{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}=\frac{\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\;\;\text{ and }

\displaystyle\zeta_{k+1,k+1}^{v}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\widehat{\bm{v}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\frac{\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\,\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}.

(47d)

Finally, a fixed-point solution
(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1})
must also satisfy Eq. (
24
), in that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}L^{h}_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+L^{h}_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\xi}_{h}\right)-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\zeta_{k+1,\ell}^{u}\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\zeta_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}

\displaystyle=\nabla(\phi^{u}_{k+1})(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1};\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})\text{ and }

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}L^{g}_{k+1,\ell}\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+L^{g}_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\xi}_{g}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\zeta_{k+1,\ell}^{v}\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\zeta_{k+1,k+1}^{v}\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{SE},*}

\displaystyle=\nabla(\phi^{v}_{k+1})(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1};\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}).

Substituting Eq. (
47
) above and noting that
\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}=L^{g}_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\xi}_{g}=\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{g}
, the first display reads as

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\Bigg(\underbrace{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{h}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{h}}_{\mathsf{h}[\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}]}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{u}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{t}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}}=\nabla(\phi^{u}_{k+1})(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1};\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}).

(48a)

Proceeding in an identical manner for the second display after noting that

\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}=L^{h}_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\xi}_{h}=\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{h},

we have

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{g}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\bm{\xi}_{g}}_{\mathsf{g}[\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}]}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}

\displaystyle\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\bm{v}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\nabla(\phi^{v}_{k+1})(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1};\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}).

(48b)

Comparing Eq. (
48
) with Eq. (
30
) and applying Lemma
4.1
, we see that
(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1})
is a solution to the Hilbert saddle problem (
29
). The construction of relations above confirm how the tuple
(\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\widehat{\bm{v}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\widehat{\bm{u}}{}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1})
can be obtained from
(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},*}_{k+1})
via Eq. (
32
).
∎

5
Proof of Theorem
3.2
: Finite sample deviation around SE

Let us begin by recalling some definitions for convenience. Recall from Eq. (
14
) that

\bm{g}_{k}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{v}_{k}\bigr)_{k,\ell}\bm{v}_{\ell}-\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}\quad\text{and}\quad\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}_{k}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k}\bigr)_{k,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}+\bm{X}\bm{v}_{k},

so that
\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{v}_{k}\bigr)_{k,\ell}\bm{v}_{\ell}-\bm{g}_{k}
and similarly
\bm{X}\bm{v}_{k}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}_{k}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k}\bigr)_{k,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}
. Also, since
\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}
consists of jointly Gaussian vectors (and analogously
\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}
), we have the decomposition
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\alpha_{k,\ell}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}+\alpha_{k,k}\bm{\xi}_{g}
and
\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\beta_{k,\ell}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}+\beta_{k,k}\bm{\xi}_{h}
for deterministic scalars
\{\alpha_{k,\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
and
\{\beta_{k,\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
constructible from
\bm{K}^{g}_{k}
and
\bm{K}^{h}_{k}
, respectively, as well as standard Gaussians
\bm{\xi}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{d}/d)
and
\bm{\xi}_{h}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{n}/n)
chosen independently of the past.
Also recall the pseudo-Lipschitz functions
\psi_{d}
and
\psi_{n}
from the statement of the theorem.

Let

\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}:=\sigma\bigl(\{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\}\bigr)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}:=\sigma\bigl(\{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\}\bigr)

denote the
\sigma
-algebras generated by the state evolution at time
k
. The following structural lemma regarding the state evolution is also a key cog that will be used in the proof.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\tau^{v}:\mathbb{R}^{d\times T}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times T}
and
\tau^{u}:\mathbb{R}^{n\times T}\times\mathbb{R}^{n\times T}
be order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz with constant
L
. For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k<T
, there exists a
\mathcal{V}_{T-k}^{\mathrm{SE}}
-measurable function
\zeta^{v}_{k}:\mathbb{R}^{d\times(T-k)}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times(T-k)}
and a
\mathcal{U}_{T-k}^{\mathrm{SE}}
-measurable function
\zeta^{u}_{k}:\mathbb{R}^{n\times(T-k)}\times\mathbb{R}^{n\times(T-k)}
such that

\displaystyle\zeta_{k}^{v}(\bm{A},\bm{B})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tau^{v}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-k}=\bm{A},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-k}=\bm{B}\bigr],\;\;\text{ a.s., }

\displaystyle\zeta_{k}^{u}(\bm{C},\bm{D})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tau^{u}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-k}=\bm{C},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-k}=\bm{D}\bigr],\;\;\text{ a.s., }

and
\zeta_{k}^{v},\zeta_{k}^{u}
are order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz with constant
C_{\mathrm{SE},T}>0
which depends only on the state evolution and the total number of iterations
T
.

Lemma
5.1
is proved in Section
5.5.1
.

With this technical lemma in hand, we begin by postulating a stronger induction hypothesis in Proposition
5.2
, from which we prove Theorem
3.2
.
We first define a collection of relevant events. We begin by defining the scalar
\Delta_{0}(\delta):=1+\sqrt{\Lambda}+\sqrt{\frac{2\Lambda\bigl[T\log(3+6T)+\log(1/\delta)\bigr]}{n}}
and the sequence of scalars

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)

\displaystyle:=C_{\mathrm{SE}}\bigl(k!\bigr)^{2}\cdot\Bigl(\frac{T\log(3+6T)+\log(\frac{1}{\delta})}{n}\Bigr)^{2^{-k}}\quad\text{ for }\quad k\in[T],

(49)

where
C_{\mathrm{SE}}
is constant depending purely on the state evolution quantities.

Our events are then defined as:

\displaystyle\Omega_{op}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\Delta_{0}(\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{vv}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bigl\|\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k}\bigr]-\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{gv}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bigl\|\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k}\bigr]-\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{gg}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bigl\|\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k}\bigr]-\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{uu}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bigl\|\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\bigr]-\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{hu}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bigl\|\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\bigr]-\langle\!\langle\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{hh}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bigl\|\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\bigr]-\langle\!\langle\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{\psi_{d}}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{\psi_{n}}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{k}^{\phi^{v}}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\max_{\ell\in T}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\Bigr\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\},

\displaystyle\ \Omega_{k}^{\phi^{u}}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\max_{\ell\in T}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\Bigr\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\}

Note that Lemma
5.1
guarantees that the conditional expectations above are all well-defined measurable functions.

Our aforementioned stronger claim is then given by the following proposition.

Proposition 5.2
.

With the events defined above, let
\Omega_{k}=\Omega^{op}\cap\Omega_{k}^{vv}\cap\Omega_{k}^{gv}\cap\Omega_{k}^{gg}\cap\Omega_{k}^{uu}\cap\Omega_{k}^{hu}\cap\Omega_{k}^{hh}\cap\Omega_{k}^{\psi_{d}}\cap\Omega_{k}^{\psi_{n}}\cap\Omega_{k}^{\phi^{u}}\cap\Omega_{k}^{\phi^{v}}
. Then for each
k\in[T]
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})\leq\frac{2\delta\cdot 3^{k-1}}{3^{T}-1}.

Let us confirm that Proposition
5.2
implies the claimed theorem.
By definition, on event
\Omega_{T}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\psi_{d}(\bm{V}_{T},\bm{G}_{T})-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(T,\delta)\quad\text{ and }\quad\bigl\lvert\psi_{n}(\bm{U}_{T},\bm{H}_{T})-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(T,\delta).

Additionally, the event
\Omega_{k}
occurs with probability at least
1-\delta
for each
k\in[T]
, by Proposition
5.2
. Applying the proposition for
k=T
thus proves Theorem
3.2
.

We are now ready to proceed to the proof of Proposition
5.2
, by induction.

5.1
Base case:
k=1

First note that by, e.g.,
Wainwright 2019
, the event
\Omega_{op}
holds with probability at least
1-\delta/\{T\log(3+6T)\}
.
By Eq. (
3.1
), we have
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{u}_{1}
and
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{v}_{1}
. Furthermore, since
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=0
and
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=0
, we have
L^{v}_{1}=L^{u}_{1}=0
. Consequently, we have

\bm{g}_{1}=-\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{u}_{1}
and
\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}_{1}=\bm{X}\bm{v}_{1}
.
Since
\bm{u}_{1}
and
\bm{v}_{1}
are chosen independently of
\bm{X}
, we have
\bm{g}_{1}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\frac{\|\bm{u}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}}{p}\bm{I}_{p})
and
\bm{h}_{1}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\frac{\|\bm{v}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}\bm{I}_{n})
. Thus, we have
(\bm{g}_{1},\bm{v}_{1},\bm{h}_{1},\bm{u}_{1})\overset{(d)}{=}(\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1},\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1})
.
We establish that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1}^{vv})\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{10(3^{T}-1)}
and , noting that similar arguments can be used to control the probability of the other events by the same value. Taking a union bound then proves the base case.

Since
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{v}_{1}
by construction, conditioning on the event
\{\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{v}_{1}\}
is trivial and we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{v}_{1},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{g}_{1}\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{g}_{1}\bigr].

(50)

But each entry of the matrix on the RHS, by Lemma
5.1
, is an order-2 pseudo-Lipschitz function of
\bm{g}_{1}
. Thus, for each each
1\leq\ell\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq T
,
Lemma
C.5
applied in conjunction with Bernstein’s inequality
(see, e.g.,
Vershynin 2018
, Theorem 2.8.1)
yields

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{g}_{1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|\leq\Delta_{1}(1,\delta)\quad\text{ with probability }\quad\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{10T^{2}\log(3+6T)}.

(51)

Putting together Eqs. (
50
) and (
51
) with a union bound over the
\binom{T}{2}
entries in the matrix, we obtain that with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{10T^{2}\log(3+6T)}
, the following deviation bound holds:

\bigl\|\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k}\bigr]-\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(1,\delta).

This completes the proof of the base case.

5.2
Induction hypothesis

We now explicitly state the induction hypothesis and collect some facts that are guaranteed by it.
The induction hypothesis is that
\Omega_{k^{\prime}}
occurs with high probability for all
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
.
In particular, on
\cap_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k}\Omega_{k^{\prime}}
, we have (among other bounds) that for all
k^{\prime}\leq k
:

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{V}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{G}_{k^{\prime}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52a)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{V}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{G}_{k^{\prime}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52b)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{V}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{G}_{k^{\prime}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52c)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{U}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{H}_{k^{\prime}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52d)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{U}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{H}_{k^{\prime}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52e)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{U}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{H}_{k^{\prime}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52f)

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{V}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{G}_{k^{\prime}}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52g)

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{U}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{H}_{k^{\prime}}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52h)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in T}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{V}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{G}_{k^{\prime}}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta),

(52i)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in T}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{U}_{k^{\prime}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}=\bm{H}_{k^{\prime}}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta).

(52j)

Setting
\ell=\ell^{\prime}=k^{\prime}
above, we obtain that on
\Omega_{k}

|\|\bm{u}_{k^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}]|\;\vee\;|\|\bm{v}_{k^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}]|\;\vee\;|\|\bm{g}_{k^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}]|\;\vee\;|\|\bm{h}_{k^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}]|\leq\Delta_{1}(k^{\prime},\delta).

Since the state evolution quantities themselves are bounded and we have
n
sufficiently large, this implies that on
\Omega_{k}
,

\displaystyle\max(\|\bm{u}_{k^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2},\|\bm{v}_{k^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2},\|\bm{g}_{k^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2},\|\bm{h}_{k^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2},\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2},\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2},\|\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2},\|\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{2})\lesssim 1\quad\text{for all }k^{\prime}\in[k].

(53)

Let us now turn to listing a key result that is guaranteed on event
\Omega_{k}
.
The following useful lemma that decomposes
\bm{X}
at iteration
k
, allowing us to approximate the projection of the data matrix
\bm{X}
onto the subspace spanned by the previous iterates. The lemma is proved in Section
5.5
. Define the pair of matrices

\displaystyle\bm{T}_{g}:=\bm{G}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}(\bm{U}_{k})^{\top}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\bm{T}_{h}:=\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}(\bm{V}_{k})^{\top}.

(54)

Lemma 5.3
.

Consider the random matrix
\bm{X}^{\parallel}=\bm{X}-\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{X}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}
. There exists a constant
C_{\mathrm{SE}}>0
, depending only on the state evolution quantities, such that on the event
\Omega_{k}
, we have

\Big\|\bm{X}^{\|}-(-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}+\bm{T}_{h})\Big\|_{{\rm op}}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

where
\bm{T}_{g}
and
\bm{T}_{h}
are defined in Eq. (
54
).
Moreover, if
\widetilde{\bm{X}}
denotes an independent copy of the data matrix
\bm{X}
, then
\bm{X}\;|\;\bm{U}_{k},\bm{V}_{k}\overset{d}{=}\bm{X}^{\parallel}+\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\widetilde{\bm{X}}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}
.

5.3
Proof of induction step for first order update

We first require a preliminary lemma. Recall the coefficient matrices
(\bm{L}^{u}_{\ell})_{\ell=1}^{T},(\bm{L}^{v}_{\ell})_{\ell=1}^{T}
that define expansions of
(\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})
, and through them
(\bm{h}_{\ell},\bm{g}_{\ell})
. Also recall the scalars
(\alpha_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}},\beta_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}})_{1\leq\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell\leq T}
defining expansions of
(\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})_{\ell=1}^{T}
. The following fact guarantees some useful relations between these coefficients, the matrices
\bm{T}_{g},\bm{T}_{h}
, and the iterates.

Lemma 5.4
.

On the event
\Omega_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}\overset{(a)}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),\;\;

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{v}_{\ell}-\bm{T}_{h}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}\overset{(b)}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

(55)

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}-\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}\overset{(a)}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),\quad

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\alpha_{k+1,\ell}\bm{g}_{\ell}-\bm{T}_{g}\bm{u}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}\overset{(b)}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

(56)

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}-\beta_{k+1,k+1}\Bigr\rvert\overset{(a)}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),\quad

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}-\alpha_{k+1,k+1}\Bigr\rvert\overset{(b)}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

(57)

We must show that
\Omega_{k+1}
occurs with high probability when iteration
k
is a first-order method. In particular, when
(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})
are generated by a GFOM, we must show that

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

(58a)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

(58b)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

(58c)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

(58d)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

(58e)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

(58f)

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

(58g)

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta).,

(58h)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in T}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

(58i)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in T}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta).

(58j)

Fix
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]
. By Lemma
5.1
, the
\mathbb{R}^{n\times(k+1)}\times\mathbb{R}^{n\times(k+1)}\to\mathbb{R}
maps

\displaystyle\tau^{uu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:(\bm{C},\bm{D})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{C},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{D}]

(59a)

\displaystyle\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:(\bm{C},\bm{D})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{C},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{D}]

(59b)

\displaystyle\tau^{hh,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}:(\bm{C},\bm{D})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{C},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{D}]

(59c)

\displaystyle\tau^{\psi_{n},k+1}:(\bm{C},\bm{D})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{C},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{D}],

(59d)

\displaystyle\tau^{\phi^{u}_{\ell},k+1}:(\bm{C},\bm{D})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\phi^{u}_{\ell}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{C},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{D}]\text{ for all }\ell\in[T],

(59e)

all exist, and are pseudo-Lipschitz. Analogously define the
\mathbb{R}^{d\times(k+1)}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times(k+1)}\to\mathbb{R}
pseudo-Lipschitz maps
\tau^{vv,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}},\tau^{gv,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}},\tau^{gg,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}},\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1},\tau^{\phi^{v}_{\ell},k+1}
.
We establish Eq. (
58
) by making use of the pseudo-Lipschitz property of these maps. In particular, we carry out the argument for establishing Eq. (
58e
), noting that all the other arguments can be carried out in the same fashion.

Write
\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k+1})=\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}_{k+1})
for convenience. To establish Eq. (
58e
), we must bound the quantity

\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})\right\rvert
by
\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)
with high probability. Toward that end, we use the triangle inequality to decompose the desired quantity as

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\quad\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right|+\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

(60)

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we have used the induction hypothesis (
52e
). We now make the following (deterministic) claim regarding state evolution — we prove the claim at the end of the section.

Claim 2
.

If iteration
k
is a first-order update, then
(L^{u}_{k+1})_{k+1,k+1}=(L^{v}_{k+1})_{k+1,k+1}=0
.

Note that if the
k
-th step is first-order, then we have

\bm{u}_{k+1}=f^{u}_{k+1}(\bm{X}\bm{v}_{k};\bm{U}_{k})\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}f^{u}_{k+1}\Bigl(\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}_{k}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell};\bm{U}_{k}\Bigr)

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows by definition of
\bm{h}_{k}
and as a result of Claim
2
.
At the same time, by definition of the state evolution for a GFOM we have

\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=f^{u}_{k+1}(\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}-\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k};\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})=f^{u}_{k+1}\Bigl(\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell};\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\Bigr).

\displaystyle\text{(From these expressions, the following bound can also be verified):}\qquad\qquad\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}\vee\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}.

(61)

Consequently, we have the equivalence of events
\{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\}=\{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\}
, and thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]=\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}].

(62)

Note that by definition of
\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]=\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}_{k+1}).

(63)

Putting together Eqs. (
60
), (
62
) and (
63
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|\geq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right\rvert\geq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)-\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\}\cap\Omega_{k}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2\delta\cdot 3^{k-1}}{3^{T}-1}.

It remains to bound the probability on the RHS.
Recall the quantity
\bm{X}^{\|}
from Lemma
5.3
. Applying this together with Claim
2
yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}_{k+1}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k+1}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}+\bm{X}\bm{v}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}+\bm{X}\bm{v}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}+\bm{X}^{\parallel}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{X}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}.

Recalling the approximation matrices
\bm{T}_{g},\bm{T}_{h}
from Eq. (
54
), define the quantity
\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.23611pt]{4.3629pt}{0.43057pt}}{\bm{h}}_{k+1}
and the random variable
\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1}
via the relations

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.23611pt]{4.3629pt}{0.43057pt}}{\bm{h}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{X}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1},

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}\bm{\gamma}_{n},

where
\bm{\gamma}_{n}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_{n}/n)
is independent of everything else. Note, by Lemma
5.3
, on
\Omega_{k}
,

\|\bm{h}_{k+1}-\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.23611pt]{4.3629pt}{0.43057pt}}{\bm{h}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\quad\text{ and }\quad\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\makebox[0.0pt][l]{\hskip 2.08334pt\hskip 0.0pt\rule[8.23611pt]{4.3629pt}{0.43057pt}}{\bm{h}}_{k+1}\overset{(d)}{=}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\widetilde{\bm{X}}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1},

where
\widetilde{\bm{X}}
is an independent copy of
\bm{X}
. Moreover,
\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\widetilde{\bm{X}}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}\overset{(d)}{=}\sqrt{\Lambda}\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{\gamma}_{n}
. Thus, we set

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}=\frac{20T^{2}\delta 3^{k-1}}{3^{T}-1}

(64)

and note that, by rotational invariance of the Gaussian distribution, Bernstein’s inequality, and Lemma
5.4
, on
\Omega_{k}
, the event

\Omega^{\prime}:=\Bigl\{\sqrt{\Lambda}\bigl\|\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\bigr\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{k\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}\Bigr\}\quad\text{ satisfies }\quad\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime})\geq 1-\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{10T^{2}}.

Thus, there exists a coupling of
\widetilde{\bm{X}}
and
\bm{\gamma}_{n}
such that on
\Omega^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{h}_{k+1}-\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq kC_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{k\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}kC_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

(65)

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from our choice of
\delta^{\prime}
since
n
is large enough.
Note that the constants
C_{\mathrm{SE}}
may be different in the two instances but depend only on the state evolution quantities.

Moreover, note from Eq. (
53
) and and Eq. (
61
) that
\max_{k^{\prime}\in[k]}\|\bm{h}_{k}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
and
\max_{k^{\prime}\in[k+1]}\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}\lesssim 1
. Since
\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
is pseudo-Lipschitz in its final argument, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}_{k+1})-\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim k\cdot\|\bm{h}_{k+1}-\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}k^{2}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

(66)

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from Eq. (
65
).
Putting the pieces together yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right\rvert\geq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)-\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\}\cap\Omega_{k}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right\rvert\geq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)-\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\}\cap\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\Bigr)+\mathbb{P}\{(\Omega^{\prime})^{c}\}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right\rvert\geq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)-k^{2}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\}\cap\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+\delta^{\prime}/(10T^{2})

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right\rvert\geq\frac{2\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)}{3}\Bigr\}\cap\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\Bigr)+\delta^{\prime}/(10T^{2}),

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from Eq. (
66
) and step
{\sf(ii)}
from the definition of
\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)
, taking
C_{\mathrm{SE}}
to be large enough.

The final step is to relate
\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1}
to another RV that resembles
\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}
conditional on the event
\{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}=\bm{H}_{k}\}
. Using the shorthand
\rho_{k}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}
,
\sigma_{k}=\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}_{k+1}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}
and
\epsilon_{k}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\Bigl(\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}-\beta_{k+1,k+1}\Bigr)\bm{\gamma}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1}=\rho_{k}+\sigma_{k}+\epsilon_{k}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\beta_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\gamma}_{n},

But by Lemma
5.4
, we have
\|\rho_{k}\|_{2}\vee\|\sigma_{k}\|_{2}\vee\|\epsilon_{k}\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)
. Setting
\bm{h}^{\mathsf{surr}}_{k+1}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}+\beta_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\gamma}_{n}
, we obtain

\|\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1}-\bm{h}^{\mathsf{surr}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq kC_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

Invoking pseudo-Lipschitzness of
\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
as before, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\widetilde{\bm{h}}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right\rvert\geq\frac{2\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)}{3}\Bigr\}\cap\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}^{\mathsf{surr}}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right\rvert\geq\frac{2\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)}{3}-k^{2}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\}\cap\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\left\lvert\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{h}^{\mathsf{surr}}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]\right\rvert\geq\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)}{3}\Bigr)

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we once again use the definition of
\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)
, taking the defining constant
C_{\mathrm{SE}}
to be large enough.

Moreover, by definition of the state evolution, we have

\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}+\beta_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\gamma}_{n}
,
and so by definition of
\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
we have

\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}]=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}\Bigl[\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\Bigl(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}+\beta_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\Bigr)\Bigr].

Consequently, by definition of
\bm{h}^{\mathsf{surr}}_{k+1}
we must bound the tails of the random variable

\displaystyle R:=\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\Bigl(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}+\beta_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\Bigr)-\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}\Bigl[\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\Bigl(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{H}_{k},\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}+\beta_{k+1,k+1}\bm{\gamma}_{n}\Bigr)\Bigr].

But
\tau^{hu,k+1}_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}
is pseudo-Lipschitz in
\bm{\gamma}_{n}
, so applying Lemma
C.5
in conjunction with Bernstein’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl(|R|>C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{k\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}\biggr)\leq\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{10T^{2}}.

Hence, since
\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)\geq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{k\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}
, combining the elements gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|\geq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)\Bigr)\leq\frac{2\delta^{\prime}}{10T^{2}}+\frac{2\delta 3^{k-1}}{3^{T}-1}.

Taking a union bound, and recalling the definition of
\delta^{\prime}
in Eq. (
64
) yields the desired result.

Proof of Claim
2
.

The proof proceeds by induction. For the base case, note that by definition,
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=0
. Suppose now that the claim holds for all
\ell\in[k]
. Note that the state evolution implies that we have

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}},\quad\text{ for all }\quad\ell\in[k+1].

(67)

Moreover, by the state evolution, there is a unique
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}
which satisfies the above relation. It thus suffices to show that there exist constants
\{c_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}
such that
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}c_{\ell}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}
. Expanding the first
k
equations in (
67
) and letting
\bm{c}=[c_{1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;c_{k}]
yields
\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bm{c}=\langle\!\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}
so that by Assumption
2
,
\bm{c}=(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}
. We now check that this choice of
\bm{c}
satisfies the final relation
\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}
. To this end, we recall that, by joint Gaussianity, we can find scalars so that
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\alpha_{k+1}\bm{\xi}_{g}+\widetilde{\bm{g}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}
, for some
\widetilde{\bm{g}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\in\text{span}\{\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2},\ldots,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\}
and where
\bm{\xi}_{g}
is independent of
\{\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1},\ldots,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\}
. Hence, since
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}
is independent of
\bm{\xi}_{g}
, we have
\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\widetilde{\bm{g}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\rangle_{L^{2}}
. Letting
\bm{w}=[(\bm{K}^{g}_{k+1})_{k+1,1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;(\bm{K}^{g}_{k+1})_{k+1,k}]\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, we have

\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bm{c},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{c}=\bm{w}^{\top}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}.

The proof is complete upon noting that
\widetilde{\bm{g}}_{k}^{\mathrm{SE}}=\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\bm{w}
and executing an identical argument for
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}
.
∎

5.4
Proof of the induction step for saddle point updates

We must verify the deviation bounds in Eq. (
58
). To be concrete, throughout this section, we will consider
\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}
(
59
), noting that an identical argument holds for each of the other functions. Since

\mathbb{E}[\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})]=\mathbb{E}[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})],

it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1},\bm{G}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}_{d}\left(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\right)\right\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta).

(68)

The proof for saddle point updates is not as explicit as that for first order updates. To set up the proof, recall that the iterates
(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})
are identified as the unique saddle point of the update function

\displaystyle F_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}):=\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{v}-\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u})+\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}).

(69)

Proof roadmap:

The proof proceeds in several steps — we first sketch these steps before presenting them in detail below. As in the proof for first order updates, a key barrier to showing deviation results of the form (
68
) is
the dependence between the previous iterates
(\bm{u}_{\ell},\bm{v}_{\ell})_{\ell=1}^{k}
and the data matrix
\bm{X}
. Towards unraveling this dependence, we first apply Lemma
5.3
to decompose—in step 0—the data
\bm{X}
into a component determined by the past iterates and an orthogonal component, allowing us to write an alternative strongly convex-strongly concave function
L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}
that—uniformly over a constant sized ball—approximates
F_{k+1}
.
Recalling the definitions of
\bm{g}_{k+1}
and
\bm{h}_{k+1}
for saddle updates, and noting that
\nabla_{v}F_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})=0
and
\nabla_{u}F_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})=0
, we see that

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k+1}

\displaystyle:=\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k+1}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{v}_{k}-\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{u}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k+1}\bm{v}_{k+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{v}_{k}+\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}_{k+1})\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}_{k+1}

\displaystyle:=\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k+1}\bm{u}_{k+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{k}+\bm{X}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k+1}\bm{u}_{k+1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{k}+\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u}_{k+1}).

This motivates the definition of

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v})

\displaystyle:=\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k+1}\bm{v}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{v}_{k}+\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v})\quad\text{ and }

(70a)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}_{k+1}(\bm{u})

\displaystyle:=\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k+1}\bm{u}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{k}+\nabla\phi^{u}_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1}).

(70b)

Writing
(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1})
as the unique saddle of
L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}
and taking
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}=\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}),\bm{h}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}=\bm{h}_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1})
, we note that in order to establish Eq. (
68
), it thus suffices to show that

\left\lvert\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{G}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}_{d}\left(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\right)\right\rvert\lesssim\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta).

Instead of directly bounding this quantity, we note that if these deviations are large, then two saddle values obtained by restrictions on the function
L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}
must obey certain properties. The problem is thus reduced to controlling the probability of these events by analyzing the restricted saddle objectives of
L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}
. This proof strategy is occasionally referred to in the literature as a local stability argument
(
Miolane and Montanari 2021
)
.

In step 1, we reduce the problem of analyzing the restricted saddle objectives of
L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}
to analyzing the restricted saddle objectives of a different loss function
\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}
, which in the typical terminology of the CGMT is known as the auxiliary loss. Unlike
L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}
, the auxiliary loss may not be convex-concave, and therefore does not have a well-defined saddle point. Thus, the previous proof technique of reduction to studying deviations around a surrogate saddle point faces a roadblock.
To circumvent this, we invoke an idea of
Celentano and Wainwright 2023
and introduce approximate stationary points
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
and
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
in step 2, proving certain properties of these points along the way.
In step 3, we show that the minmax
value
of
\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}
is very close to pure maximization (respectively minimization) of
\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})
and
\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{v})
over
\bm{u}
(respectively
\bm{v}
).
We conclude the proof in step 4 by considering each of the functions (
59
) to control every required deviation in the inductive hypothesis. Applying a union bound over each of these and taking the probability of error suitably small to ensure the geometric series sums to
\delta
after
T
iterations then yields the inductive step for a saddle update.

Step 0:

Recall the approximation matrices
\bm{T}_{g},\bm{T}_{h}
(
54
). In order to interpret these matrices, we define

\displaystyle\bm{G}_{k}^{\perp}

\displaystyle:=\bm{G}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{g}\bigr)^{-1/2},\qquad\bm{H}_{k}^{\perp}:=\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{h}\bigr)^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\bm{U}_{k}^{\perp}

\displaystyle:=\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{g}\bigr)^{-1/2},\qquad\bm{V}_{k}^{\perp}:=\bm{V}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{h}\bigr)^{-1/2},

noting that on the event
\Omega_{k}
, the columns of each of the four matrices defined above are approximately orthonormal by an argument similar to Lemma
5.4
. With these definitions, we have
\bm{T}_{g}=\bm{G}_{k}^{\perp}\bigl(\bm{U}_{k}^{\perp}\bigr)^{\top}
and
\bm{T}_{h}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{H}_{k}^{\perp}\bigl(\bm{V}_{k}^{\perp}\bigr)^{\top}
.

Lemma
5.3
then inspires us to define the primary objective
4
4

4

We emphasize to the reader that here we use the terminology primary objective in anticipation of our application of the CGMT
(
Thrampoulidis et al. 2018
)
in the sequel.

L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}):=\bm{u}^{\top}\Bigl(\bm{T}_{h}^{\top}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}+\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\widetilde{\bm{X}}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\Bigl)\bm{v}-\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u})+\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v})

(71)

and its (unique) saddle point as
