(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1})=\mathsf{saddle}\{L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}
.

Note that the primary objective approximates the update function
F_{k+1}
. Indeed, by Lemma
5.3
, on
\Omega_{k}
, we have the uniform bound

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(M),\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(M^{\prime})}\;\Bigl\lvert L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})-F_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\Bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE},M,M^{\prime}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

where
M,M^{\prime}>0
are small enough constants. The following lemma, whose proof we provide in Section
B.2
, is a straightforward corollary of this uniform approximation.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
F_{k+1}
be as in (
69
) and let
(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})
denote its unique saddle point. There exist universal constants
c_{\mathrm{SE}},C_{\mathrm{SE}}>0
such that if

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(M),\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(M^{\prime})}\;\Bigl\lvert L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})-F_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\Bigr\rvert\leq C_{M,M^{\prime}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

then

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}\vee\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq c_{\mathrm{SE}},\quad\text{ and }\quad\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}-\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}\vee\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}-\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot\sqrt{k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)}.

(72)

Invoking the order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz nature of
f_{d}
and
f_{n}
in conjunction with the bound
\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)\geq\sqrt{k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)}
yields

\left\lvert\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{G}_{k})-\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1},\bm{G}_{k})\right\rvert\leq\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)}{2}.

Hence, to establish Eq. (
68
), it suffices to show that

\left\lvert\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{G}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})\right\rvert\leq\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)}{2}.

To this end, we define deviation sets

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{d}(\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1})

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}}):\,\left\lvert\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}),\bm{G}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})\right\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)\Bigr\},

and study both the unrestricted behavior of the loss
L_{k+1}^{\mathrm{PO}}
as well as the behavior of the loss when its arguments are restricted to the deviation sets. To do so, we define the quantity
\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}
as the value of the auxiliary state evolution saddle point problem in Eq. (
27
). That is,

\displaystyle\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}:=\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{d}}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{n}}\;\big\{\mathfrak{L}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\big\}.

Equipped with this notation, we define pair of events
\Omega_{1}^{\mathrm{PO}}
and
\Omega_{2}^{\mathrm{PO}}
as

\displaystyle\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;\bigl\{L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\bigr\}<\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\}\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{D}_{d}^{c}(f_{d})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;\bigl\{L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\bigr\}>\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)^{2}}{k^{2}}\Bigr\}.

In words,
\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{1}
encodes the event that the unrestricted saddle point has value bounded above by
\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)
and
\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{2}
encodes the event that the value of the saddle problem when the minimization variable is restricted to the set
\mathbb{D}_{d}^{c}
is bounded below by
\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)^{2}}{k^{2}}
. Since
\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)/k\asymp\sqrt{\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)}
, this implies that on the intersection
\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{1}\cap\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{2}
, we have
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}\in\mathbb{D}_{d}(\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1})
. Formally, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl\{\left\lvert\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{G}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k})\right\rvert>\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl\{(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{1})^{c}\cup(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{2})^{c}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl\{(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{1})^{c}\bigr\}+\mathbb{P}\bigl\{(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{2})^{c}\bigr\}.

In the rest of the proof, we show that the probability of each of the events
(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{1})^{c}
and
(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{2})^{c}
is small.

Step 1: Reduction to the auxiliary objective via the CGMT.

To bound the probability of our events, the direct objects to analyze
are the (restricted) saddles of
L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}
. However, this object is complex because the loss is defined by a random matrix. To simplify the loss under consideration, we define a closely related auxiliary objective. Towards defining the auxiliary objective, let
\bm{\gamma}_{d}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_{d})
and
\bm{\gamma}_{n}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_{n})
be independent of all other randomness, and define the auxiliary objective
\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}):=

\displaystyle-\Bigl(\bm{T}_{g}\bm{u}+\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{d}}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigr)^{\top}\bm{v}

\displaystyle+\Bigl(\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)^{\top}\bm{u}-\phi^{u}_{k+1}(\bm{u})+\phi^{v}_{k+1}(\bm{v}).

(73)

We bound the probability of our events of interest by relating them to the auxiliary loss
\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}
, using the convex Gaussian minmax theorem. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl\{(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{1})^{c}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\{L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}\geq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr\}\bigcap\Omega_{k}\Bigr)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\{L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}\geq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\leq}2\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\{\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}\geq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})

(74a)

\displaystyle=:2\mathbb{P}\Bigl((\Omega^{\mathsf{AO}}_{1})^{c}\Bigr)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c}).

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from Lemma
5.5
from step 1 and step
{\sf(ii)}
follows from the first part of the CGMT (see, e.g.,
Miolane and Montanari 2021
or
Chandrasekher et al. 2023
). Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl\{(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{2})^{c}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{D}_{d}^{c}(\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\{L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}\leq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)^{2}}{k^{2}}\Bigr\}\bigcap\Omega_{k}\biggr)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{D}_{d}^{c}(\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\{L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}\leq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)^{2}}{k^{2}}\Bigr)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}2\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{D}_{d}^{c}(\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\{\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}\leq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)^{2}}{k^{2}}\Bigr)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})

(74b)

\displaystyle=:2\mathbb{P}\Bigl((\Omega^{\mathsf{AO}}_{2})^{c}\Bigr)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c}),

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from the second part of the CGMT (see, e.g.,
Miolane and Montanari 2021
or
Chandrasekher et al. 2023
). It thus suffices to the probabilities of the events
(\Omega^{\mathsf{AO}}_{1})^{c}
and
(\Omega^{\mathsf{AO}}_{2})^{c}
, which involve studying (restricted) saddles of the auxiliary loss.
While the auxiliary loss has the advantage that there is no longer a random matrix involves, it is no longer convex-concave. Accordingly, to understand its restricted saddle values, we will construct
approximate
stationary points of the auxiliary loss.

Step 2: Approximate stationary points and their properties.

Before defining the approximate stationary points, we require some preliminary notation. Recalling our invertibility assumption on
\bm{K}^{g}_{k}
and
\bm{K}^{h}_{k}
, define functions
\bm{g}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}:\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}:\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\bm{h}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}:\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}:\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
as

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{B},\bm{w})

\displaystyle:=\bm{B}\cdot\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1/2}\bigl\langle\!\bigl\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1/2},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr\rangle\!\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\bm{w},

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{E},\bm{z})

\displaystyle:=\bm{E}\cdot\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1/2}\bigl\langle\!\bigl\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1/2},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr\rangle\!\bigr\rangle_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\bm{z},

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}(\bm{A})

\displaystyle:=\bm{A}\cdot\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\Bigl\{\langle\!\langle\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}-L^{v}_{k+1,k+1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{u}}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}(\bm{D})

\displaystyle:=\bm{D}\cdot\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\Bigl\{\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}-L^{u}_{k+1,k+1}\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\}.

Use these objects to define the functions
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}:\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}:\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
as

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}(\bm{A},\bm{B},\bm{w})=\argmin_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\biggl\{\frac{L^{v}_{k+1,k+1}}{2}\Bigl\|\bm{v}-(L^{v}_{k+1,k+1})^{-1}\cdot\bigl[\bm{g}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}(\bm{B},\bm{w})-\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}(\bm{A})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}+\phi^{v}_{k+1}(\bm{v})\biggr\},

(75a)

and

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}(\bm{D},\bm{E},\bm{z})=\argmin_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\biggl\{\frac{L^{u}_{k+1,k+1}}{2}\Bigl\|\bm{u}-(L^{u}_{k+1,k+1})^{-1}\cdot\bigl[\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{h}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}(\bm{E},\bm{z})-\widehat{\bm{u}}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}(\bm{D})\bigr]\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}+\phi^{u}_{k+1}(\bm{u})\biggr\}.

(75b)

Note that we have

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d})\quad\text{ and }\quad\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n}/\sqrt{n})

(76)

for
\bm{\gamma}_{d}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{d})
and
\bm{\gamma}_{n}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{n})
.
To exploit these relations to the SE, we work with a different evaluation of these functions, which we will then show are approximate stationary points of the auxiliary loss. Abusing notation slightly, define

\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}:=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d})

according to Eq. (
75a
) and

\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}:=\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n}/\sqrt{n})

according to Eq. (
75b
), where
\bm{\gamma}_{d}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{d})
and
\bm{\gamma}_{n}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{n})
.
The following lemma establishes several useful concentration properties of the approximate stationary points, and parallels guarantees in Proposition
5.2
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\bm{V}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}=[\bm{V}_{k}\;|\;\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}]
,
\bm{U}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}=[\bm{U}_{k}\;|\;\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}]
,
\bm{G}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}=[\bm{G}_{k}\;|\;\bm{g}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}]
, and
\bm{H}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}=[\bm{H}_{k}\;|\;\bm{h}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}]
. If
\delta\in(e^{-c_{\mathrm{SE}}n},1)
, then with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}-\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})
, the following hold simultaneously.

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77a)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77b)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77c)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77d)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77e)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[T]}\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}]-\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\,\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell^{\prime}}\rangle]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77f)

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{d}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77g)

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi_{n}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\bigr]\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77h)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in T}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{G}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}},

(77i)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in T}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{H}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}\bigr]-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}}.

(77j)

Step 3: Relating auxiliary saddle value to evaluations on approximate stationary points.

We now consider evaluations of the auxiliary objective at the approximate stationary points
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
and
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
.
Toward this end, we define the pair of functions
G:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
H:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle G(\bm{v})

\displaystyle:=\bm{v}^{\top}\Bigl(-\bm{T}_{g}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\bm{T}_{h}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{d}}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}\|_{2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})+\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}),

(78a)

\displaystyle H(\bm{u})

\displaystyle:=\bm{u}^{\top}\Bigl(\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}\|_{2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u})+\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}).

(78b)

Importantly, by Claim
1
,
\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\geq 0
and
\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\geq 0
so that
G
is strongly convex and
H
is strongly concave. Also, by construction, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})

\displaystyle=H(\bm{u})+\Bigl(\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}\|_{2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{d}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigr),\text{ and }

(79a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{v})

\displaystyle=G(\bm{v})-\Bigl(\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}\|_{2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr).

(79b)

We will show shortly (invoking Lemma
5.6
) that the terms in brackets above are appropriately small, so that
H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})
and
G(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})
carry (approximate) information about the desired restricted saddle values.
The following lemma (proved in Section
B.4
) shows properties of
H
and
G
at
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
and
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
respectively.

Lemma 5.7
.

If
\delta^{\prime}\in(e^{-c_{\mathrm{SE}}n},1)
, then with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}-\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})
, the following hold simultaneously

\displaystyle\lvert G(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})-\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}\quad\text{ and }

(80a)

\displaystyle\|\partial G(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}.

(80b)

\displaystyle\lvert H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})-\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}\quad\text{ and }

(80c)

\displaystyle\|\partial H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}.

(80d)

We are now in a position to bound the probabilities of
(\Omega_{1}^{\mathrm{AO}})^{c}
and
(\Omega_{2}^{\mathrm{AO}})^{c}
. The following facts about the state evolution quantities will be useful in the proof. Recall that
\bm{\xi}_{g}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{d}/d)
and
\bm{\xi}_{h}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{n}/n)
; we have

\displaystyle\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\langle\!\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}},

(81a)

\displaystyle\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\langle\!\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}}.

(81b)

These relations can be directly obtained from the definition of the state evolution and Eq. (
26
); also see Eq. (
97
) to follow.

Let
\Omega^{\prime}
denote the event that all the inequalities in Lemmas
5.6
and
5.7
hold with parameter
\delta^{\prime}
so that
\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\prime})\geq 1-\delta^{\prime}-\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})
and let
\Omega_{0}^{\prime}=\bigl\{\|\gamma_{n}\|_{2}\vee\|\gamma_{d}\|_{2}\leq C_{\Lambda}\sqrt{d}\bigr\}
, which satisfies
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{0}^{\prime})\geq 1-e^{-cn}
.

Bounding
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{1}^{\mathrm{AO}})^{c}
.

Note that by definition of
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{d}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}}{\sqrt{d}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bigl(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\bigl(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{G}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}}.

We also have
\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}}
. But
by Lemma
5.6
, on
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle|\bigl(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\langle\!\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}}

\displaystyle\|\bigl(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{G}_{k}-\langle\!\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{k}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+\sqrt{k}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{10T^{2}}{\delta^{\prime}})}{n}}.

Putting together the pieces, we obtain

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{d}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}}{\sqrt{d}}-\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\rvert\vee\bigl\lvert\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\bigr\rvert\leq\sqrt{k}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+\sqrt{k}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}.

(82)

Now note that on the event
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\bigl\{

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\bigr\}\leq\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\bigl\{\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\Bigl\{H(\bm{u})+\Bigl(\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}\|_{2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{d}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\leq}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\bigl\{H(\bm{u})\bigr\}+\sqrt{k}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+\sqrt{k}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

(83)

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from Eq. (
79a
) and
step
{\sf(ii)}
from the inequality (
82
).
Hence, invoking the concavity of
H
in conjunction with the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we deduce that, for any
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(K_{2})
,

H(\bm{u})\leq H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}})+\partial H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}})^{\top}(\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}})\overset{\text{Lemma}~\ref{lem:approximate-stationarity-F-func}}{\leq}\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}.

Combining the elements yields that on
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\bigl\{\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\bigr\}\leq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\{\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}\geq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\Bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{(\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{\prime})^{c}\right\}\leq\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c}).

(84)

Bounding
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{2}^{\mathrm{AO}})
.

In order to reduce the notational burden, we set

\Delta:=2C_{\mathrm{SE}}^{\prime}k\cdot\biggl\{k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log\bigl(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime}\bigr)}{n}}\biggr\}^{1/2}=2C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta),

which is by assumption larger than the right-hand side of the inequalities in Lemma
5.6
. We thus have, on
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{d}^{c}(\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1})

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}}):\;\bigl\lvert\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}),\bm{G}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}\bigl(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr)\bigr]\bigr\rvert>\Delta\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\subseteq}\Bigl\{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}}):\;\bigl\lvert\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}(\bm{v},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}),\bm{G}_{k})-\tau^{\psi_{d},k+1}\bigl(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}),\bm{G}_{k}\bigr)\bigr\rvert>\Delta/2\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\subseteq}\Bigl\{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}}):\;\|\bm{v}-\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}>\frac{\Delta}{kc_{\mathrm{SE}}}\Bigr\},

where the inclusion
{\sf(i)}
follows from Lemma
5.6
(in particular the inequality (
77h
)) and the inclusion
{\sf(ii)}
follows from the order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz nature of
\tau^{\psi_{n},k+1}
. Consequently, on
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{D}_{d}^{c}}\bigl\{\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\bigr\}\geq\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{D}_{d}^{c}}\bigl\{\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{v})\bigr\}\geq\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}}):\;\|\bm{v}-\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}>\frac{c_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta}{k}}\bigl\{\mathfrak{L}^{\mathrm{AO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{v})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{=}\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}}):\;\|\bm{v}-\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}>\frac{c_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta}{k}}\Bigl\{G(\bm{v})-\Bigl(\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}\|_{2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{h},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\geq}\max_{\bm{v}\in\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}}):\;\|\bm{v}-\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}>\frac{c_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta}{k}}\bigl\{V(\bm{v})\bigr\}+\sqrt{k}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+\sqrt{k}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

(85)

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from Eq. (
79b
) and step
{\sf(ii)}
from an argument identical to the one above but using Eq. (
81b
).
Invoking the strong convexity of
G
in conjunction with the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle G(\bm{v})

\displaystyle\geq G(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})+\partial G(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})^{\top}(\bm{v}-\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})+\frac{\mu}{2}\|\bm{v}-\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{Lemma}~\ref{lem:approximate-stationarity-F-func}}{\geq}\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}-C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)-C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}+\frac{\mu}{2}\|\bm{v}-\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{\Delta^{2}}{k^{2}},

where the final inequality follows upon taking
C_{\mathrm{SE}}^{\prime}
in the definition of
\Delta
large enough. Consequently, we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\min_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{D}_{d}}\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\{\mathfrak{L}_{n}^{(k+1)}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{\Delta_{1}(k+1,\delta)^{2}}{k^{2}}\Bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{(\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{\prime})^{c}\right\}\leq\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c}).

(86)

Step 4: Putting the pieces together.

Combining the inequalities (
74a
), (
74b
), (
84
), and (
86
), we find that

\mathbb{P}(\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{1}\cap\Omega^{\mathsf{PO}}_{2})\geq 1-\delta^{\prime}-3\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})
.
Taking

\delta^{\prime}=\frac{2\delta\cdot 3^{k-1}}{10T^{2}\cdot(3^{T}-1)},

we conclude by specifying every function
\tau
in (
59
) and repeating the previous step (all on the event
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{0}^{\prime}
) so that

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k+1}^{c})\leq 2\delta^{\prime}+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{k}^{c})\leq\frac{2\delta\cdot 3^{k}}{3^{T}-1},

as desired. ∎

5.5
Proofs of helper lemmas

In this section, we collect proofs of Lemmas
5.1
and
5.3
, which were used in the proof of Theorem
3.2
before the inductions steps.

5.5.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

The first part follows from the definition of conditional expectation, so we restrict ourselves to proving pseudo-Lipschitzness. Note that the case
k=0
holds by assumption, so we begin with the case
k=1
. We have two cases, when the final step is a first-order update and when the final step is a saddle update.

Case 1: The final step is first-order.
In this case, we have

\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}=f_{T}^{v}\Bigl(\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}c_{\ell}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\Bigr)\quad\text{ and }\quad\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}=\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},

for some constants
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{T}
and
c_{1},\ldots,c_{T-1}
, where
\widetilde{\bm{g}}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_{d}/d)
is independent of everything else. Hence,

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}^{v}(\bm{A},\bm{B})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tau^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}=\bm{A},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}=\bm{B}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\tau^{v}\Bigl(f_{T}^{v}\Bigl(\bm{b}_{T-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}c_{\ell}\bm{a}_{\ell}\Bigr),\bm{A},\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{b}_{T-1}\Bigr)\Bigr],

where the expectation is taken with respect to
\widetilde{\bm{g}}
. Invoking the pseudo-Lipschitz nature of
\tau^{v}
in conjunction with the Lipschitz nature of
f_{T}^{v}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}^{v}(\bm{A},\bm{B})-

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}^{v}(\bm{A}^{\prime},\bm{B}^{\prime})=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\tau^{v}\Bigl(f_{T}^{v}\Bigl(\bm{b}_{T-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}c_{\ell}\bm{a}_{\ell}\Bigr),\bm{A},\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{b}_{T-1}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt-\tau^{v}\Bigl(f_{T}^{v}\Bigl(\bm{b}_{T-1}^{\prime}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}c_{\ell}\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\Bigr),\bm{A}^{\prime},\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{b}_{T-1}^{\prime}\Bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl\{1+\Bigl\|f_{T}^{v}\Bigl(\bm{b}_{T-1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}c_{\ell}\bm{a}_{\ell}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{2}+\Bigl\|f_{T}^{v}\Bigl(\bm{b}_{T-1^{\prime}}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}c_{\ell}\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{2}+\|\widetilde{\bm{g}}\|_{2}+

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\|\bm{a}_{\ell}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|_{2}+\|\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}\Bigr\}\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\|\bm{a}_{\ell}-\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}-\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl\{1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\|\bm{a}_{\ell}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|_{2}+\|\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}\Bigr\}\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\|\bm{a}_{\ell}-\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}-\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2},

where in the final inequality we used the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{g}}\|_{2}]\lesssim 1
. This concludes the proof in the case when the last step is first-order.

Case 2: The final step is saddle update.
By definition, there exist scalars
c_{1},\ldots,c_{T}
such that
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{T}c_{\ell}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}
and
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{T}
such that
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}=\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}
. It follows from (
10
) that
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}
admits the equivalent representation

\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}=\argmin_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\;\Bigl\{\frac{c_{T}}{2}\cdot\Bigl\|\bm{v}-\frac{1}{c_{T}}\cdot\Bigl(\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\beta_{\ell}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}c_{\ell}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\Bigr)\Bigr\|_{2}^{2}+\phi^{v}_{T}(\bm{v})\Bigr\}.

Hence, by
Bauschke and Combettes 2017
,
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}
is a Lipschitz function of
\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1},\ldots,\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}
and
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1},\ldots,\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}
. For notational convenience, we let
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times(T-1)}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times(T-1)}
denote this function so that
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T}=\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1})
. We thus have

\displaystyle\zeta^{v}_{1}(\bm{A},\bm{B})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tau^{v}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}=\bm{A},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}=\bm{B}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\tau^{v}\Bigl(\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}),\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1},\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}\Bigr)\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}=\bm{A},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1}=\bm{B}\Bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\tau^{v}\Bigl(\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{A},\bm{B}),\bm{A},\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{b}_{\ell},\bm{B}\Bigr)\Bigr],

where the first equality is almost sure and the expectation in the final relation is only with respect to
\widetilde{\bm{g}}
. Invoking the pseudo-Lipschitz nature of
\tau^{v}
in conjunction with the Lipschitz nature of
\eta
yields

\displaystyle\zeta^{v}_{1}(\bm{A},\bm{B})-

\displaystyle\zeta^{v}_{1}(\bm{A}^{\prime},\bm{B}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\tau^{v}\Bigl(\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{A},\bm{B}),\bm{A},\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{b}_{\ell},\bm{B}\Bigr)-\tau^{v}\Bigl(\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{A}^{\prime},\bm{B}^{\prime}),\bm{A}^{\prime},\alpha_{T}\widetilde{\bm{g}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\alpha_{\ell}\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime},\bm{B}^{\prime}\Bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\Bigl\{1+\|\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{A},\bm{B})\|_{2}+\|\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{A}^{\prime},\bm{B}^{\prime})\|_{2}+\|\widetilde{\bm{g}}\|_{2}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\|\bm{a}_{\ell}\|_{2}+\|\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\Bigl\{\|\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{A},\bm{B})-\eta(\widetilde{\bm{g}},\bm{A}^{\prime},\bm{B}^{\prime})\|_{2}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\|\bm{a}_{\ell}-\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}-\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}\Bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigl\{1+\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\|\bm{a}_{\ell}\_2+\|\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}\Bigr\}\cdot\sum_{\ell=1}^{T-1}\|\bm{a}_{\ell}-\bm{a}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2}+\|\bm{b}_{\ell}-\bm{b}_{\ell}^{\prime}\|_{2},

where the final inequality again uses
\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\bm{g}}\|_{2}]\lesssim 1
.

Conclusion for
k>1
.
To conclude for
k>2
, note that

\displaystyle\zeta_{2}^{v}(\bm{A},\bm{B})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tau(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-2}=\bm{A},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-2}=\bm{B}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\zeta_{1}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-2},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-2})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-2}=\bm{A},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{T-2}=\bm{B}\bigr],

and proceed inductively since
\zeta_{1}^{v}
is pseudo-Lipschitz.
∎

5.5.2
Proof of Lemma
5.3

In parallel with the SE notation, define

\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{v}_{k}\bigr)_{k,\ell}\bm{v}_{\ell}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\bm{u}}_{k}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k}\bigr)_{k,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell},

and stack these as columns to form the matrices
\widehat{\bm{V}}_{k}
and
\widehat{\bm{U}}_{k}
, respectively.

Recall that
\bm{X}^{\parallel}=\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{X}+\bm{X}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}-\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{X}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}
, and that
\bm{T}_{g}=\bm{G}_{k}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}=\widehat{\bm{V}}_{k}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}-\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{U}_{k}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}
and
\bm{T}_{h}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{H}_{k}(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}=\bm{X}\bm{V}_{k}(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}-\widehat{\bm{U}}_{k}(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}
, where we have used the definitions (
14
) of
\bm{G}_{k}
and
\bm{H}_{k}
. We thus have

\bigl\|\bm{X}^{\parallel}-(-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}+\bm{T}_{h})\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq I.+II.+III.,

where

\displaystyle I.

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{X}-\bm{U}_{k}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{X}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}},\quad II.=\bigl\|\bm{X}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}-\bm{X}\bm{V}_{k}(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\quad III.=\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{X}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}-\bigl(\widehat{\bm{U}}_{k}(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}-\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\widehat{\bm{V}}_{k}\bigr)\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

We bound each term in turn, beginning with term
I.
To this end, we note that by the Woodbury matrix identity, for two invertible matrices
\bm{A},\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, the following holds

\displaystyle\|\bm{A}^{-1}-\bm{B}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|\bm{B}^{-1}(\bm{A}-\bm{B})\bm{A}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{A})\lambda_{\min}(\bm{B})}\bigl\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k}{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{A})\lambda_{\min}(\bm{B})}\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{\infty}.

(87)

Hence, since
\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}=\bm{U}_{k}(\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{U}_{k})^{-1}\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}
, we deduce that on
\Omega_{k}
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{X}-\bm{U}_{k}\bm{K}_{g}^{-1}(\bm{U}_{k})^{\top}\bm{X}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{0}(k,\delta)\cdot\|\bm{U}_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\bigl\|(\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{U}_{k})^{-1}-(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{k\cdot\|\bm{U}_{k}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\Delta_{0}(k,\delta)\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)}{\lambda_{\min}\bigl(\bigl\{\bigl(\bm{U}_{k}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{U}_{k}\bigr\}^{-1}\bigr)\lambda_{\min}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\frac{k\cdot\|\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\Delta_{0}(k,\delta)\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\bm{K}^{g}_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{0}(k,\delta)\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from Eq. (
87
) and step
{\sf(ii)}
follows by taking
c_{\mathrm{SE}}
in the assumed lower bound on
\delta
small enough and applying the eigenvalue stability inequality
(e.g.,
Tao 2012
, Eq. (1.63))
. Term
II.
can be bounded in an identical manner, so we omit the details for brevity.

Turning to term
III.
, we expand

\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{X}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}=\bm{U}_{k}(\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{U}_{k})^{-1}\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{V}_{k}(\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{V}_{k})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}.

Since by definition (
14
),
\bm{X}\bm{V}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}-\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{L}_{k}^{u}\bigr)^{\top}
, we see that on
\Omega_{k}
,

\bigl\|\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{V}_{k}-\bigl\{\bigl(\bm{L}_{k}^{v}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{K}^{h}_{k}-\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bm{L}_{k}^{u}\bigr\}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

Further, following the same steps as in the bound on term
I.
yields the pair of inequalities

\displaystyle\bigl\|(\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{U}_{k})^{-1}-(\bm{K}^{g}_{h})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\bigl\|(\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{V}_{k})^{-1}-(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

Combining these three inequalities along with the assumption
\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)\leq T^{-1}
yields the inequality

III.\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

Combining the pieces yields the desired result. The second part of the lemma follows from Lemma
C.4
.
∎

6
Discussion

In this paper, we provided a rigorous state evolution for the class of saddle point updates (possibly interleaved with first-order updates) as well as finite-sample guarantees. We hope that the tools introduced in this paper both for proving uniqueness of state evolution in nonseparable settings and finite-sample concentration results will find broader application in studying the dynamics of learning algorithms. Multiple intriguing open questions remain and we detail a few here.

First, we made simplifying assumptions in this paper that we believe can be weakened. For instance, it would be of interest to consider penalty functions in our saddle updates that are not smooth and strongly convex. Second, while we gave an explicit description of the state evolution for a large class of methods, we did not use the state evolution to derive any optimization-theoretic consequences.
This has been the focus of some previous work in the sample-split setting
(
Chandrasekher et al. 2023
)
, and it would be interesting to provide similar characterizations without sample-splitting. Indeed, our companion paper makes progress in this direction for some concrete iterative algorithms.
Third, we restricted our attention to the setting in which the entries of the data matrix
\bm{X}
are standard Gaussian. It is known in the literature on AMP and first-order methods that these predictions are to some extent universal
(
Chen and Lam 2021
;
Han 2024
;
Lovig et al. 2025
)
. Given the universality of
M
-estimators
(
Han and Shen 2023
, see, e.g.,)
under delocalization assumptions (see also
Oymak and Tropp 2018
), we expect such results to hold for the broader class of iterations considered here and it would be interesting to show such a result rigorously.
Fourth, and as previously mentioned, non-asymptotic guarantees for saddle updates suffer from a known drawback of working directly with loss functions in the CGMT
(
Miolane and Montanari 2021
)
. It was shown in, e.g.,
Chandrasekher et al. 2024
that, for a class of sample-split algorithms, the leave-one-out method of
Bean et al. 2013
;
El Karoui 2013
can be utilized to provide sharper non-asymptotic guarantees. It would be interesting to prove such results in the present case, without sample-splitting. The finite-sample deviation rate also currently scales as
C_{\mathrm{SE}}(T!)^{2}
. While we argued that this may be impossible to substantially improve for general algorithms, it would be interesting to establish faster concentration results for specific first-order and saddle algorithms.
Fifth, in the context of mean-field spin glasses, lower bounds on the performance of iterative methods have been shown for large classes of algorithms
(
Huang and Sellke 2025
)
. It would be interesting to study the relationship between this class of algorithms and our saddle updates, and to use this understanding to design optimal families of saddle updates. Finally, it is of interest to go beyond the saddle update formalism to encompass more methods, such as those in numerical optimization
(
Conn et al. 2000
)
and semiparametric inference
(
Kakade et al. 2011
;
Pananjady and Foster 2021
)
. This would likely require significantly different techniques from those introduced in the present paper.

Appendix

Appendix A
Examples illustrating parameterization of state evolution

A.1
Recovering AMP state evolution

Consider the expanded AMP dynamics in Eq. (
12
). Note that the corresponding state evolution given by Eqs. (
3.1
)–(
11
) is

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\qquad

and

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=\bm{y}_{2k}^{\mathrm{SE}}+\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}+\lambda_{k-1}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1},

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+1}=\lambda_{k}\cdot\bigl(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}-\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}+\Lambda\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\bigr)\qquad

and

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+1}=\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},

where
\bm{y}_{2k}^{\mathrm{SE}}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\sigma^{2}\bm{I}_{n})
, independent of everything else with initialization
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{v}_{1}
,
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\bm{u}_{1}
,
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\mathsf{N}(0,\|\bm{v}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\bm{I}_{d}/d)
,
\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=\mathsf{N}(0,\|\bm{u}_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\bm{I}_{n}/n)
,
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=0
, and
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}=0
. We claim that
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+2}=\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+1}=-\lambda_{k}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}
and
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+1}=\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=-\Lambda\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}
. Since
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}=\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-2}
and
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}
, this implies that

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\qquad

and

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=\bm{y}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1},

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+1}=-\lambda_{k}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\qquad

and

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+1}=\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}.

We additionally take
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\tau_{k}^{2})
, independent of all other randomness, and
\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+1}=\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}
as well as
\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k+1}\sim\mathsf{N}\bigl(0,\lambda_{k}^{2}\cdot\{\sigma^{2}+\Lambda\tau_{k-1}^{2}\}\bigr)
, independent of all other randomness, and
\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}
where
\tau_{k}^{2}=\sigma^{2}+\Lambda\lambda_{k-1}^{2}\tau_{k-1}^{2}
.

Let us now verify that these choices verify the fixed point equations in Eq. (
11
). We proceed by induction. Note that the base case holds by construction as the fixed point equations are indeed verified at the first iteration. Suppose that it holds for all positive integers
\ell\leq 2k-1
. From the induction hypothesis, since
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}
and
\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}=\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}
, we see that
\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\!\langle\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}
. Note further that

\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{y}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1},\bm{y}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sigma^{2}+\Lambda\|\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\sigma^{2}+\Lambda\lambda_{k-1}^{2}\tau_{k-1}^{2}=\tau_{k}^{2},

which is equivalent to
\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle_{L^{2}}
by construction. Moreover, for any
\ell\leq 2k-1
, we have

\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{y}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{y}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\rangle=0,

where the final inequality follows from independence. By construction, we see that
\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
, so that
\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}
. Additionally, we have, for each
\ell\leq k
that

\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},-\lambda_{\ell-1}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell-2}\rangle_{L^{2}}=-\lambda_{\ell-1}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell-2}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}},

and for each
\ell\leq k-1
that

\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},-\lambda_{\ell}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=-\lambda_{\ell}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}.

Thus,
\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\widehat{\bm{U}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}
. It remains to verify that
\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\!\langle\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\widehat{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}
. We have

\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1},\bm{y}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=-\Lambda\|\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

On the other hand,

\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1},-\Lambda\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=-\Lambda\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-\Lambda\|\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k-1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, for each
\ell\leq k-1
, we have

\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2\ell+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}.

A similar set of calculations then verifies that
\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\!\langle\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}=\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k},\widehat{\bm{V}}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{2k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}
, as desired.

A.2
Rigorous state evolution for one step of CGMT

In this section, we use the state evolution in Eqs. (
8
)–(
11
) to recover the nonlinear system in
Thrampoulidis et al. 2018
(see also
Bellec and Koriyama 2023
). We are interested in regularized
M
-estimation problems where we minimize the loss

\displaystyle L({\widetilde{\bm{v}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\rho(\bm{y}-\bm{X}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}})+\frac{\lambda}{d}f({\widetilde{\bm{v}}})=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho(y_{i}-\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}})+\frac{\lambda}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}f(\widetilde{v}_{j})

where

\rho
is smooth and separable and
f
is strongly convex and separable.
For simplicity of exposition, we will consider the setting in which the observations
\bm{y}=0
(with true signal equal to
0
), whereby we obtain

\displaystyle L({\widetilde{\bm{v}}})

\displaystyle=\overset{(d)}{=}\frac{1}{d}\rho(\bm{X}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}})+\frac{\lambda}{d}f({\widetilde{\bm{v}}})=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\rho(\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}{\widetilde{\bm{v}}})+\frac{\lambda}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}f(\widetilde{v}_{j}).

Note that our normalization matches that of
Thrampoulidis et al. 2018
. Dualizing and scaling the decision variables so that
\bm{v}={\widetilde{\bm{v}}}/\sqrt{d}
, we obtain the equivalent representation

L(\bm{v})=\sup_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\;\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{v}-\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial\rho^{\star}\bigl(u_{j}\sqrt{d}\bigr)+\frac{\lambda}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}f\bigl(v_{j}\sqrt{d}\bigr),

where
\rho^{\star}
denotes the convex conjugate of
\rho
. Note that the minimizer
\bm{v}^{\mathsf{opt}}
of this loss can be written in conjunction with the maximizing
\bm{u}^{\mathsf{opt}}
as

(\bm{u}^{\mathsf{opt}},\bm{v}^{\mathsf{opt}})=\underset{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathsf{saddle}}\Big\{\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{v}-\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial\rho^{\star}\bigl(u_{j}\sqrt{d}\bigr)+\frac{\lambda}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}f\bigl(v_{j}\sqrt{d}\bigr)\Big\}.

The state evolution from Eqs. (
8
)–(
11
) now applies, and yields

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}-\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigl[\partial\rho^{\star}\bigl(u^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}\sqrt{d}\bigr)\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\partial\rho^{\star}\bigl(u^{\mathrm{SE}}_{n}\sqrt{d}\bigr)\bigr]^{\top}\quad\text{ and }

(88a)

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}-\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{d}}\bigl[\partial f\bigl(v^{\mathrm{SE}}_{1}\sqrt{d}\bigr)\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\partial f\bigl(v^{\mathrm{SE}}_{d}\sqrt{d}\bigr)\bigr]^{\top}.

(88b)

Using the span constraints to take
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}}=\kappa\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}
,
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}=\nu\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}
and letting
\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\beta^{2}
, and
\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\alpha^{2}
then simplifies the fixed point equations to

\displaystyle\beta^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\qquad

\displaystyle\alpha^{2}=\|\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(88c)

\displaystyle\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{SE}},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\kappa\beta^{2},\qquad

\displaystyle\langle\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}},\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\nu\alpha^{2}.

We next show that the system of equations (
88
) is equivalent to the system of equations in
Bellec and Koriyama 2023
; see Eq. (
13
).
Let us show equivalence of Eqs. (
13
) with our state evolution (
88
) by considering each equation in turn. We begin with Eq. (
13a
) and define the random variable
V^{\mathrm{BK}}:=\mathsf{prox}_{\lambda\nu^{-1}f}(\nu^{-1}\beta Z)
so that
V^{\mathrm{BK}}
satisfies the KKT condition

\nu V^{\mathrm{BK}}-\beta Z+\lambda\partial f(V^{\mathrm{BK}})=0.

We recognize from (
88b
) that, since
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}=\kappa\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}
,

\nu v^{\mathrm{SE}}_{j}-g^{\mathrm{SE}}_{j}+\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{d}}\partial f\bigl(v^{\mathrm{SE}}_{j}\sqrt{d}\bigr)=0\quad\text{ for all }j\in[d].

It thus follows that the entries of
\sqrt{d}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}
are i.i.d. with the same law as
V^{\mathrm{BK}}
so that Eq. (
13a
) is equivalent to
\alpha^{2}=\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
.

We next turn to Eq. (
13b
). Note that by Moreau’s identity,

\alpha W-\mathsf{prox}_{\kappa\rho}(\alpha W)=\mathsf{prox}_{(\kappa\rho)^{\star}}(\alpha W)

Define
U^{\mathrm{BK}}:=\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathsf{prox}_{(\kappa\rho)^{\star}}(\alpha W)
,
so that Eq. (
13b
) is equivalent to
\beta^{2}=\Lambda\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{BK}})^{2}]
. Next, noting that
(\kappa\rho)^{\star}(x)=\kappa\rho^{\star}(x/\kappa)
, we note that
U^{\mathrm{BK}}
satisfies the KKT condition

\kappa U^{\mathrm{BK}}-\alpha W+\partial\rho^{\star}\bigl(U^{\mathrm{BK}}\bigr)=0.

On the other hand, from Eq. (
88a
), we see that

\kappa u^{\mathrm{SE}}_{i}-\sqrt{\Lambda}h^{\mathrm{SE}}_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\partial\rho^{\star}\bigl(u^{\mathrm{SE}}_{i}\sqrt{d}\bigr)=0\quad\text{ for all }i\in[n].

It thus follows that the entries of
\sqrt{d}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}
are i.i.d. with the same law as
U^{\mathrm{BK}}
so that
\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\Lambda\mathbb{E}[(U^{\mathrm{BK}})^{2}]
. Hence, Eq. (
13b
) is equivalent to
\beta^{2}\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
.

We next turn to Eq. (
13c
), which is equivalent to the equation

\nu\alpha=\Lambda\mathbb{E}\bigl[WU^{\mathrm{BK}}]
. On the other hand, our state evolution implies that

\displaystyle\nu\alpha^{2}=\nu\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}},\widehat{\bm{v}}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}.

(89)

Note that the entries of
\sqrt{d\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}
are i.i.d. with the same law as
\mathsf{N}(0,\alpha^{2})
and the entries of
\sqrt{d}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}
are i.i.d. with the same law as
U^{\mathrm{BK}}
, so that

\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\Lambda\alpha\mathbb{E}[WU^{\mathrm{BK}}].

Re-arranging we thus see that Eq. (
89
) is equivalent to Eq. (
13c
).

We finally consider Eq. (
13d
). By definition, we have
\mathbb{E}\Bigl[Z\cdot\mathsf{prox}_{\lambda\nu^{-1}f}(\nu^{-1}\beta Z)\Bigr]=\mathbb{E}[ZV^{\mathrm{BK}}]
. On the other hand, since the entries of
\sqrt{d}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}
are i.i.d. with the same law as
V^{\mathrm{BK}}
and the entries of
\sqrt{d}\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}
are i.i.d.
\mathsf{N}(0,\beta^{2})
, and invoking the fixed point relation in Eq. (
88c
), we have

\kappa\beta^{2}=\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}\rangle_{L^{2}}=\beta\mathbb{E}[ZV^{\mathrm{BK}}],

which in turn is equivalent to Eq. (
13d
), as desired.

Appendix B
Proofs of technical lemmas from Section
5

In this section, we prove the technical lemmas that were stated in our proofs of the finite-sample guarantees. We first prove Lemma
5.4
, which was used in the first-order induction step, and then Lemmas
5.5
,
5.6
, and
5.7
, which were used in the saddle induction step.

B.1
Proof of Lemma
5.4

We prove each of the statements in turn, restricting our proofs to inequalities (a) in the left column; inequalities (b) admit identical proofs. Starting with inequality (
55
)(a), we expand

\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{g}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{G}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl\{\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{g}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{G}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\bigr\}_{\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}.

Since for first-order methods, the events
\{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{V}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{U}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\}
and
\{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k},\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{H}_{k}\}
are equivalent, on
\Omega_{k}
, we have

\bigl\|\bm{G}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}-\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

As in the proof of Claim
2
, we expand the state evolution equations in (
67
) to deduce that

\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}=\bm{K}^{g}_{k}[\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k}].

Putting the pieces together, we find that

\Bigl\|\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}-\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{u}_{\ell}\Bigr\|_{2}\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{g}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{G}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\bigr\}_{\ell}-\bigl(L^{u}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

as desired.

Turning to inequality (
56
)(a), we expand

\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}_{k+1}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\cdot\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl\{\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{h}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\bigr\}_{\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}.

Similar to the previous argument, we note that on
\Omega_{k}
,

\bigl\|\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}-\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\|_{\infty}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

Further, recall from the proof of Claim
2
that, for
\ell\in[k]
,
\beta_{k+1,\ell}=\bigl\{\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\}_{\ell}
. Putting the pieces together, it thus follows that

\Bigl\|\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}_{k+1}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bm{h}_{\ell}\Bigr\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\Lambda}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl\lvert\bigl\{\bigl(\bm{K}_{k}^{h}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\bigr\}_{\ell}-\beta_{k+1,\ell}\bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

as desired.

Finally, we turn to inequality (
57
)(a). We have

\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}-\langle\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1},(\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\rangle.

Note that on
\Omega_{k}
,
\lvert\|\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)
. We thus focus on the second term. As in Eq. (
87
), we note that for two invertible matrices
\bm{A},\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, it holds that

\|\bm{A}^{-1}-\bm{B}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{k}{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{A})\lambda_{\min}(\bm{B})}\|\bm{A}-\bm{B}\|_{\infty}.

Hence on
\Omega_{k}
,

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\langle\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1},(\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\rangle-\langle\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1},(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\rangle\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}\bigl\|(\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top})^{-1}-\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

Further, applying the Cauchy–Schwartz inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\langle

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1},(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\rangle-\langle\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle,(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle\rangle\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigl\|(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}-\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle\bigr\|_{2}\cdot\Bigl\{\bigl\|\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}+\bigl\|\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle\bigr\|_{2}\Bigr\}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)

Putting the pieces together, we find that

\Bigl\lvert\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}-\Bigl\{\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\langle\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle,(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle\Bigr\}\Bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

Recognizing that

\beta_{k+1,k+1}=\bm{K}^{h}_{k+1,k+1}-\bigl\langle\bigl[\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\bigr(\bm{K}^{h}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k}\bigr],(\bm{K}^{h}_{k})^{-1}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\bigr(\bm{K}^{h}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k}\bigr]\bigr\rangle,

yields the desired result.
∎

B.2
Proof of Lemma
5.5

Suppose as in the lemma that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}}),\bm{u}\in\mathbb{B}_{2}(c_{\mathrm{SE}})}\;\Bigl\lvert L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})-F_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\Bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

(90)

Note that we have the following sandwich relation:

\displaystyle\frac{\mu}{2}\|\bm{v}_{k+1}-\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}F_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1})-F(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

(91)

where the inequality
{\sf(i)}
follows from strong convexity of the map
\bm{v}\mapsto F_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v})
and the inequality
{\sf(ii)}
follows from combining the uniform bound (
90
) with the saddle relations

\displaystyle F_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})\leq F_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})\leq F_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1})\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1})\leq L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1})\leq L^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1}).

Re-arranging inequality (
91
) and repeating the same steps for
\bm{u}_{k+1}
thus yields the pair of claimed bounds

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}-\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot\sqrt{k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)}\quad\text{ and }\quad\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{k+1}-\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\cdot\sqrt{k\cdot\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)}.

(92)

It remains to prove that

\displaystyle\|\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}\vee\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq c_{\mathrm{SE}}.

(93)

Note that, since
\phi_{k+1}^{u}
is strongly convex, the function
\gamma:\bm{v}\mapsto\max_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,F_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v})
is well-defined and strongly convex as it is the maximum of strongly convex functions. Moreover, by Danskin’s theorem
[
Rockafellar 1971
, Corollary 1]
,
\nabla\gamma(\bm{v})=\bm{X}^{\top}\argmax_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\{\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u})\}+\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v})
. Thus, since
\gamma
is
\mu
-strongly convex, on
\Omega_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bm{v}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}\frac{2}{\mu}\|\nabla\gamma(0)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\mu}\cdot\bigl(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\|\argmax_{\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\{\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u})\}\|_{2}+\|\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v}(0)\|_{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\mu}\cdot\bigl(\Delta_{0}(k,\delta)+K\bigr)\leq c_{\mathrm{SE}}^{\prime},

(94)

where in step
{\sf(i)}
we have used the fact that for a differentiable,
\mu
-strongly convex function
f:\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}
,
\|\argmin_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{m}}\,f(x)\|_{2}\leq\frac{2}{\mu}\|\nabla f(0)\|_{2}
and the final inequality follows from Assumption
1
. Continuing, since
(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})
is a saddle point of
F_{k+1}
, it satisfies the sandwich relation

F_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}_{k+1})\leq F(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})\leq F(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}),\quad\text{ for all }\quad\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Taking
\bm{u}=0
in left-hand side of the sandwich relation above and invoking strong concavity of the map
\bm{u}\mapsto F_{k+1}(\bm{u},\bm{v}_{k+1})
yields

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq F_{k+1}(0,\bm{v}_{k+1})-F_{k+1}(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})\geq-\nabla_{\bm{u}}F_{k+1}(0,\bm{v}_{k+1})^{\top}\bm{u}_{k+1}+\frac{\mu}{2}\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq-\|\nabla_{\bm{u}}F_{k+1}(0,\bm{v}_{k+1})\|_{2}\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}+\frac{\mu}{2}\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}^{2}\geq-\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}\cdot\bigl(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}+\|\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u}(0)\|_{2}\bigr).

Re-arranging and invoking Assumption
1
in conjunction with the bound on
\|\bm{v}_{k+1}\|_{2}
(
B.2
) then implies that
\|\bm{u}_{k+1}\|_{2}\leq c_{\mathrm{SE}}
, for a small enough constant
c_{\mathrm{SE}}>0
. We then take
(M,M^{\prime})
in the uniform bound (
90
) to be
(c_{\mathrm{SE}},c_{\mathrm{SE}}^{\prime})
, as desired. ∎

B.3
Proof of Lemma
5.6

Each of the proofs follows a similar template and proceeds by using a pseudo-Lipschitz property, so we restrict ourselves to the proof of (
77i
). We will exploit Eq. (
76
), reproduced below for convenience:

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d})\quad\text{ and }\quad\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n}/\sqrt{n})

for
\bm{\gamma}_{d}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{d})
and
\bm{\gamma}_{d}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,\bm{I}_{n})
.

Proof of the inequality (
77i
).

For
\ell\leq k
, on
\Omega_{k}
,

\bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}_{\ell})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{V}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{G}_{k},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})\bigr]-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}_{\ell})]\bigr\rvert\leq\Delta_{1}(\ell,\delta)\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta),

so that it suffices to consider
\ell>k
. In this case, Lemma
5.1
implies that the function
\tau_{\ell}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
, defined as

\tau_{\ell}(\bm{c},\bm{C},\bm{d},\bm{D}):=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\mid\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{C},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{c},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}=\bm{D},\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{d}\bigr]

is order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz. Recalling the definition of
\tau^{\phi_{\ell}^{v},k}
(
59
), we deduce that on
\Omega_{k}

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\tau_{\ell}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}),\bm{G}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\lvert\tau_{\ell}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}),\bm{G}_{k})-\tau^{\phi_{\ell}^{v},k}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k})\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\bigl\lvert\tau^{\phi_{\ell}^{v},k}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k})-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{\ell})\bigr]\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\lvert\tau_{\ell}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}),\bm{G}_{k})-\tau^{\phi_{\ell}^{v},k}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k})\bigr\rvert+\Delta_{1}(k,\delta).

Now, recalling the definition of
\bm{g}_{k+1}(\cdot)
from (
70
), we have

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})

\displaystyle=\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d}))

\displaystyle=\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,k+1}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d})+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\bigl(L^{v}_{k+1}\bigr)_{k+1,\ell}\bm{v}_{k}+\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d})).

Since by assumption
\phi_{k+1}^{v}
is smooth and
\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
is a Lipschitz function of its arguments, it follows that
\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})
is a Lipschitz function of
\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k}
, and
\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d}
. Hence,
\tau_{\ell}
is an order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz function of
\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d}
. Also note that due to the equivalence (
76
), we have

\tau^{\phi_{\ell}^{v},k}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\gamma}_{d}}[\tau_{\ell}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d}),\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d})),\bm{G}_{k})].

Thus, applying Lemma
C.5
yields

\bigl\lvert\tau_{\ell}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1},\bm{V}_{k},\bm{g}_{k+1}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}),\bm{G}_{k})-\tau^{\phi_{\ell}^{v},k}(\bm{V}_{k},\bm{G}_{k})\bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},\quad\text{ with probability }\quad\geq 1-\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{10T^{2}}.

Putting the pieces together yields the desired result.

To conclude, we note that there are
6T^{2}+2T+2\leq 10T^{2}
events to be controlled, and applying a union bound completes the proof. ∎

B.4
Proof of Lemma
5.7

We first recall the Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{n}
and
\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{d}
from Eq. (
25
) as well as the orthonormal collections
\{\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}\in\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{d},\{\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}\in\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{n},\{\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}\in\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{d},
and
\{\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}\in\mathcal{H}^{k+1}_{n}
and collect them to form

\displaystyle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}

\displaystyle:=[\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}],\quad\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}:=[\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}],

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}

\displaystyle:=[\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}],\quad\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}:=[\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{1}\;|\;\cdots\;|\;\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}].

Note further that by assumption, the matrices
\bm{K}^{g}_{k},\bm{K}^{h}_{k}
are invertible and act as whitening transformations, so that we have the identities

\displaystyle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}

\displaystyle=\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1/2},\quad\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}=\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}

\displaystyle=\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1/2},\quad\text{ and }\quad\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}=\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1/2},

(95)

where these matrix multiplications should be understood as acting on the matrices realized by the random vectors. We will additionally exploit Eq. (
76
), i.e., the fact that

\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{d}/\sqrt{d})\quad\text{ and }\quad\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n}/\sqrt{n}).

Equipped with these relations, we prove each part of the lemma in turn.

Proof of the inequality (
80c
).

We expand

\displaystyle H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})=\Bigl\{

\displaystyle\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\frac{\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle-\Bigl\{\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\}-\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})+\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}),

and

\mathfrak{L}_{k+1}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})=-\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})]+\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})].

Hence, by the triangle inequality,

\bigl\lvert H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})-\mathrm{OPT}_{k+1}\bigr\rvert=\bigl\lvert H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})-\mathfrak{L}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})\bigr\rvert\leq T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4},

where

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\lvert\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\sqrt{\Lambda}\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\frac{\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\langle\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\rvert,

\displaystyle T_{2}
