\displaystyle=\Bigl\lvert\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\bigl\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\langle\bm{g}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\rvert,

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\lvert\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})]\Bigr\rvert\quad\text{ and }\quad T_{4}=\Bigl\lvert\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})-\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k+1}^{v}(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1})]\Bigr\rvert.

Let
\Omega^{\prime}
denote the event that each of the inequalities in Lemma
5.6
hold for parameter
\delta^{\prime}
, and for the remainder of the proof, work on the intersection
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{k}
. Note that on this intersection, from the inequalities (
77j
) and (
77i
), we have

T_{3}\vee T_{4}\leq\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}.

We claim that

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},\quad\text{ and }

(96a)

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}.

(96b)

Combining these bounds on terms
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}
, and
T_{4}
yields the desired result. It remains to establish the inequalities (
96a
) and (
96b
). The proofs are nearly identical, so we explicitly prove only inequality (
96a
).

Proof of the inequality (
96a
).

Recall from Eq. (
26
) that
\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}=\mathsf{h}[\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}]
and

\displaystyle\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\langle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{h}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{\ell}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{\xi}_{h}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\langle\!\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{\xi}_{h}\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{=}\langle\!\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{\xi}_{h}\rangle_{L^{2}},

(97)

where the final relation follows by applying the whitening transformations in Eq. (
B.4
).
On the other hand, recalling the definition of
\bm{T}_{h}
, we expand

\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}\frac{(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}=\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}\frac{(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}.

It thus suffices to show that

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr]\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr]\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}\frac{(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{\xi}_{h}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}

We handle each term in turn:

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr]\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr]\Bigr\rvert\leq A+B,

where

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\Bigl\lvert\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr]\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\Bigr\rvert,\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr]\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr]\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr]\Bigr\rvert.

On
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{k}
, we invoke the inequalities (
77a
) and (
77e
) in conjunction with the triangle inequality to deduce that both

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}_{k}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr]\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bigl(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{V}_{k}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr]\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

so that

A\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}\quad\text{ and }\quad B\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{V}_{k}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{H}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr]\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl(\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}\bigr)^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\bigr]\Bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{k}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}.

Turning to the second term, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}\frac{(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{\xi}_{h}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\rvert\leq\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\cdot\Bigl\lvert\frac{(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{\xi}_{h}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle+\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{\xi}_{h}\rangle_{L^{2}}\cdot\bigl\lvert\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\bigr\rvert+\Bigl\lvert\frac{(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})^{\top}\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\langle\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1},\bm{\xi}_{h}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\rvert\cdot\bigl\lvert\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\bigr\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}+C_{\mathrm{SE}}\bigl\lvert\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\bigr\rvert

where the final inequality follows with probability at least
1-\delta^{\prime}
by the inequality (
82
). It remains to bound the quantity
\bigl\lvert\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\bigr\rvert
. Following similar steps to establishing the bounds on
A
and
B
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\bigl\lvert\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}\bigr\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

(98)

Putting the pieces together yields the desired result.

Proof of the inequality (
80d
).

We consider two cases: when
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k+1}=0
(almost surely) and otherwise.

Case 1:
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k+1}=0
almost surely:
In this case, by Claim
1
, we see that
L^{u}_{k+1,k+1}=0
so that the stationary condition defining
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
reads

\Bigl\{\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{H}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n})-\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k})\Bigr\}+\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u}\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{H}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n})\bigr)=0.

Moreover, since by Claim
1
,
\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
, we have

\partial H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})=\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}+\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}).

Combining the previous displays and applying the triangle inequality yields

\|\partial H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})\|_{2}\leq\Bigl\|\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{H}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n}/\sqrt{n})\Bigr\|_{2}+\Bigl\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k})-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}.

We bound each of the terms on the RHS of the display above in turn. Expanding

\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}=\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle\quad\text{ and }\quad\bm{h}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{H}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n}/\sqrt{n})=\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\cdot\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{n}},

we thus have, on
\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}
that

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\bm{T}_{h}

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{H}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n}/\sqrt{n})\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\Lambda}\Bigl\|\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle-\bm{H}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}+\Bigl\lvert\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\Bigr\rvert\cdot\frac{\|\bm{\gamma}_{n}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{k\Lambda}\|\bm{H}_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\bigl(\bm{K}^{h}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\langle\!\langle\bm{V}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle-\langle\!\langle\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}+\Bigl\lvert\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}-\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\Bigr\rvert\cdot\frac{\|\bm{\gamma}_{n}\|_{2}}{\sqrt{d}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

(99)

where in the final inequality, we invoked the bounds (
77a
), (
77f
), and (
98
), which hold on
\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k})-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigl\|\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{G}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle-\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{k}\|\bm{G}_{k}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\bigl\|\langle\!\langle\bm{G}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle-\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\bigr\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

(100)

where in the final inequality, we invoked the bounds (
77b
) and (
77c
), which hold on the event
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega_{k}
. Combining the elements establishes the claim in the first case.

Case 2:
\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE},\perp}_{k+1}\|_{2}>0
, almost surely:

Note that on
\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}
,
\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}>0
. It thus follows that
F
is differentiable at
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
, and we compute

\nabla H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})=\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{V}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}-\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u}(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}).

On the other hand, from the KKT condition defining
\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}
, we have

\displaystyle L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle+L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\Bigl\{\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{H}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n})-\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k})\Bigr\}+\nabla\phi_{k+1}^{u}\bigl(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr)=0.

Adding the two preceding displays yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla H(\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl\|\bm{T}_{h}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+\|\mathsf{P}^{\perp}_{\bm{V}_{k}}\bm{v}_{k+1}^{\mathrm{apx}}\|_{2}\frac{\bm{\gamma}_{n}}{\sqrt{d}}-\sqrt{\Lambda}\bm{h}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{H}_{k},\bm{\gamma}_{n})\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigl\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k})+L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}+L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\frac{\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}}\cdot\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}+\Bigl\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k})+L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt+\Bigl\lvert L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}-\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}}\Bigr\rvert\cdot\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2},

where the final inequality follows from the bound (
99
). We next bound each of the latter two terms. Expanding, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}(\bm{U}_{k})+L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl\|\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}_{g}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{G}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}-\bm{T}_{g}^{\top}\bm{v}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigl\|L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}}

\displaystyle\quad+L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}\Bigr\|\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{U}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{U}_{k}^{\top}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}-\bm{U}_{k}\bigl(\bm{K}^{g}_{k}\bigr)^{-1}\langle\!\langle\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k},\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle\!\rangle_{L^{2}}\Bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows from the bound (
100
) and the final inequality follows on
\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}
. It remains to bound

\displaystyle\Bigl\lvert L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}-\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}}\Bigr\rvert\cdot\bigl\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl\lvert\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}}-\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}}\Bigr\rvert\cdot\|\bm{u}^{\mathrm{apx}}_{k+1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\Delta_{1}(k,\delta)+C_{\mathrm{SE}}k^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log(10T^{2}/\delta^{\prime})}{n}},

where the first inequality follows from non-expansiveness of projections as well as the representation
L_{k+1,k+1}^{u}=\frac{\langle\bm{\xi}_{g},\bm{v}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\rangle_{L^{2}}}{\|\mathsf{P}_{\bm{U}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{u}^{\mathrm{SE}}_{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}}
, and the final inequality follows on
\Omega_{k}\cap\Omega^{\prime}
. Putting the pieces together yields the desired result. ∎

Appendix C
Ancillary results

We organize this appendix into three sections.

C.1
Optimization problems on Hilbert spaces

Let
\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
denote the vector space of linear maps from
\mathbb{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{Y}
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{Y}
be separable Hilbert spaces. Further, let
f:\mathbb{X}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
g:\mathbb{Y}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be Fréchet differentiable and strongly convex. For a linear map
A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
, define the function
L:\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{Y}\times\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as
L(x,y,A):=\langle Ax,y\rangle+f(x)-g(y)
. Then, for each
A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
, there is a unique pair
(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{Y}
such that
L(\bar{x},\bar{y})=\min_{x\in\mathbb{X}}\max_{y\in\mathbb{Y}}\,L(x,y;A)
.

Proof.

Fix
A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{Y})
. Since
y\mapsto-L(x,y)
is strongly convex, it admits a unique minimizer
[
Bauschke and Combettes 2017
, Corollary 11.16]
. We thus define the function
\psi:\mathbb{X}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
as
\psi(x):=\max_{y\in\mathbb{Y}}\,L(x,y)
. Then, since
\psi
is the maximum of a collection of strongly convex functions, it is strongly convex over
\mathbb{X}
and hence admits a unique minimizer
\bar{x}:=\argmin_{x\in\mathbb{X}}\,\psi(x)
. Next, take
\bar{y}:=\argmax_{y\in\mathbb{Y}}\,L(\bar{x},y)
.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a Hilbert space equipped with the inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
that in turn induces the norm
\|\cdot\|
. Let
f:\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{R}
be some
C^{1}
function with
L
-Lipschitz gradients, in that

\displaystyle\|\nabla f(x)-\nabla f(x^{\prime})\|\leq L\|x-x^{\prime}\|\quad\text{ for all }x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}.

Suppose we have a non-expansive linear map
A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X})
and some fixed
x_{0}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\{0\}
. Let
c_{1}\geq 0
. Define the following optimization problems:

minimize

\displaystyle c_{1}\|Ax\|+f(x)\qquad

\displaystyle\text{ such that }x\in\mathbb{X}

(P1)

minimize

\displaystyle c_{1}\|Ax\|+f(x)-\langle x,\,x_{0}\rangle\quad

\displaystyle\text{ such that }x\in\mathbb{X}\text{ and }\langle x,\,x_{0}\rangle\geq 0.

(P2)

Suppose there exists a unique optimal solution
x_{1}
to problem (
P1
), and that this solution satisfies
\langle x_{1},\,x_{0}\rangle=0
.
If there exists an optimal solution
x_{2}
to problem (
P2
) satisfying
Ax_{2}\neq 0
,
then
x_{2}
must satisfy
\langle x_{2},\,x_{0}\rangle>0
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
x_{2}\neq x_{1}
. Indeed, if
x_{1}\neq x_{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\|Ax_{2}\|+f(x_{2})-\langle x_{2},\,x_{0}\rangle\overset{{\sf(i)}}{\leq}\|Ax_{1}\|+f(x_{1})\overset{{\sf(ii)}}{<}\|Ax_{2}\|+f(x_{2}),

and so
\langle x_{2},\,x_{0}\rangle>0
.
Here step
{\sf(i)}
follows because
x_{2}
is an optimal solution of problem (
P2
) and
x_{1}
is feasible for problem (
P2
) with
\langle x_{1},\,x_{0}\rangle=0
. Step
{\sf(ii)}
follows since
x_{1}
is the unique optimal solution of problem (
P1
) and
x_{2}
is feasible for problem (
P1
).
To show that
x_{2}\neq x_{1}
, we split the proof into two cases.

Case 1:
Ax_{1}=0
:

By assumption, we have
Ax_{2}\neq 0
, so we must have that
x_{2}\neq x_{1}
. This completes the proof for this case.

Case 2:
Ax_{1}\neq 0
.

First consider the subcase
c_{1}>0
. It suffices to show that there exists a feasible solution
\widetilde{x}
to the constrained problem (
P2
) such that its objective value is strictly less than the optimal objective value of the unconstrained problem (
P1
), i.e.,

\displaystyle c_{1}\|A\widetilde{x}\|+f(\widetilde{x})-\langle\widetilde{x},\,x_{0}\rangle<c_{1}\|Ax_{1}\|+f(x_{1}).

(101)

We claim that for any positive scalar
\delta<\min\Bigl\{\frac{\|Ax_{1}\|}{2\|x_{0}\|},2\bigl(\frac{2}{c_{1}\|Ax_{1}\|}+L\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr\}
, the point
\widetilde{x}=x_{1}+\delta x_{0}
is feasible for the constrained problem (
P2
) and satisfies Eq. (
101
). Indeed, feasibility follows immediately, since

\langle\widetilde{x},\,x_{0}\rangle=\langle x_{1},\,x_{0}\rangle+\delta\langle x_{0},\,x_{0}\rangle=\delta\|x_{0}\|^{2}>0.

Now note that for all
x^{\prime}
such that
\|Ax^{\prime}\|\neq 0
, we have

\left\|\nabla(\|Ax^{\prime}\|)-\nabla(\|Ax_{1}\|)\right\|=\left\|\frac{Ax^{\prime}}{\|Ax^{\prime}\|}-\frac{Ax_{1}}{\|Ax_{1}\|}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\|Ax_{1}\|\land\|Ax^{\prime}\|}\cdot\|Ax^{\prime}-Ax_{1}\|\leq\frac{1}{\|Ax_{1}\|\land\|Ax^{\prime}\|}\cdot\|x^{\prime}-x_{1}\|,

where the final inequality follows since
A
is non-expansive.
Also note that since
x_{1}
is the optimal solution to the unconstrained problem (
P1
) and
Ax_{1}\neq 0
, we have

\nabla\bigl(\|Ax\|+f(x)\bigr)\mid_{x=x_{1}}=0.

Furthermore, note that
\|A\widetilde{x}\|\geq\|Ax_{1}\|-\delta\|x_{0}\|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|Ax_{1}\|
, where the final inequality follows since
\delta<\frac{\|Ax_{1}\|}{2\|x_{0}\|}
. Thus, the function
x\mapsto\|Ax\|+f(x)
is
\{2/(\|Ax_{1}\|_{2})+L\}
-smooth on the line segment connecting
x_{1}
to
\widetilde{x}
. Using these properties in conjunction with Taylor’s theorem yields

c_{1}\|A\widetilde{x}\|+f(\widetilde{x})-\bigl\{c_{1}\|Ax_{1}\|+f(x_{1})\bigr\}\leq\left(\frac{2}{c_{1}\|Ax_{1}\|}+L\right)\cdot\frac{\|\widetilde{x}-x_{1}\|^{2}}{2}=\frac{\delta^{2}\|x_{0}\|^{2}}{2}\cdot\left(\frac{2}{c_{1}\|Ax_{1}\|}+L\right)

To verify Eq. (
101
), we may write

\displaystyle c_{1}\|A\widetilde{x}\|+f(\widetilde{x})-\langle\widetilde{x},\,x_{0}\rangle-\bigl\{c_{1}\|Ax_{1}\|+f(x_{1})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta^{2}\|x_{0}\|^{2}}{2}\cdot\left(\frac{2}{c_{1}\|Ax_{1}\|}+L\right)-\delta\|x_{0}\|^{2}\overset{{\sf(i)}}{<}0,

where step
{\sf(i)}
follows since
0<\delta<2\bigl(\frac{2}{c_{1}\|Ax_{1}\|}+L\bigr)^{-1}
.

To handle the subcase
c_{1}=0
note that
f
is differentiable at
x_{1}
and
L
-smooth. Thus, we have, for the same choice
\widetilde{x}=x_{1}+\delta x_{0}
, the inequality

f(\widetilde{x})-\langle\widetilde{x},\,x_{0}\rangle-f(x_{1})\leq\frac{L}{2}\|\widetilde{x}-x_{1}\|^{2}-\delta\|x_{0}\|^{2}=\delta\|x_{0}\|^{2}\left(\frac{L}{2}-1\right),

and the RHS is less than
0
for any
\delta\in(0,2/L)
.
∎

C.2
Saddle updates and Gaussian conditioning

Given a matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
and differentiable functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},g:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we let
I_{A}
denote the solutions to the following non-linear system of equations

\displaystyle I_{A}:=\bigl\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}:\,Ax=\nabla g(y)\text{ and }A^{\top}y=-\nabla f(x)\bigr\}.

(102)

Lemma C.3
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
g:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be differentiable and strongly convex. For each
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
, the non-linear system in (
102
) admits a unique solution
(\bar{x}(A),\bar{y}(A))
. Moreover, the map
A\mapsto(\bar{x}(A),\bar{y}(A))
is continuous.

Proof.

We first establish that the non-linear system admits a unique solution. To this end, define the function
L:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
as
L(x,y,A):=\langle Ax,y\rangle+f(x)-g(y)
. Note that, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
, the function
\phi_{x}:y\mapsto L(x,y,A)
is strongly concave and hence admits a unique maximizer so that the function
\psi:x\mapsto\max_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi(y)
is well-defined. Since
\psi
is the maximum of strongly convex functions, it is also strongly convex. Further, by Danskin’s theorem
[see, e.g.,
Rockafellar 1991
, Corollary 1]
,
\psi
is differentiable with derivative
\nabla\psi(x)=A^{\top}\cdot\argmax_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi_{x}(y)+\nabla f(x)
. Thus, since
\psi
is strongly convex and differentiable, it admits a unique minimizer
\bar{x}(A)
that satisfies
\nabla\psi\bigl(\bar{x}(A)\bigr)=0
. Similarly,
\phi_{\bar{x}(A)}
admits a unique maximizer
\bar{y}(A)
that satisfies
\nabla\phi_{\bar{x}(A)}\bigl(\bar{y}(A)\bigr)=0
. Expanding each of these first order conditions yields the non-linear system in (
102
), which by construction admits a unique solution.

We turn now to establishing continuity of the map
M:A\mapsto\bigl(\bar{x}(A),\bar{y}(A)\bigr)
. To this end, consider a sequence of matrices
(A_{k})\rightarrow A
. It suffices to show that
M(A_{\ell})\rightarrow(\bar{x},\bar{y})
. Next, let
L_{k}(x,y):=\langle A_{k}x,y\rangle+f(x)-g(y)
and
L(x,y):=\langle Ax,y\rangle+f(x)-g(y)
and note that
L_{k}(x,y)=L(x,y)+\bigl\langle(A_{k}-A)x,y\bigr\rangle
. Further, set
(\tilde{x},\tilde{y}):=M(A_{k})
. By definition, the following pair of inequalities hold for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle L_{k}(\tilde{x},y)\overset{(a)}{\leq}L_{k}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y})\overset{(b)}{\leq}L_{k}(x,\tilde{y})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad L(\bar{x},y)\overset{(c)}{\leq}L(\bar{x},\bar{y})\overset{(d)}{\leq}L(x,\bar{y}).

(103)

Consequently, taking
y=\bar{y}
in (
103
)(a),
y=\tilde{y}
in (
103
)(c) and expanding we find that

L(\tilde{x},\bar{y})+\bigl\langle(A_{k}-A)\tilde{x},\bar{y}\bigr\rangle\leq L(\bar{x},\bar{y})+\bigl\langle(A_{k}-A)\bar{x},\tilde{y}\bigr\rangle.

For each fixed
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
L(\cdot,y)
is strongly convex, so there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\frac{C}{2}\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\bigl\langle(A_{k}-A)\bar{x},\tilde{y}\bigr\rangle-\bigl\langle(A_{k}-A)\tilde{x},\bar{y}\bigr\rangle=\bigl\langle(A_{k}-A)(\tilde{x}-\bar{x}),\tilde{y}\bigr\rangle+\bigl\langle\tilde{x},(A_{k}-A)^{\top}(\tilde{y}-\bar{y})\bigr\rangle.

Re-arranging and applying Cauchy–Schwarz, we find that

\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\epsilon\cdot\bigl(\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}+\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}\cdot\|\tilde{y}-\bar{y}\|_{2}\bigr).

Repeating these steps, using the inequalities (
103
)(b,d) instead yields a similar inequality for
\|\tilde{y}-\bar{y}\|_{2}^{2}
, so that

\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\tilde{y}-\bar{y}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\epsilon\cdot\bigl(\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}+\|\tilde{y}-\bar{y}\|_{2}+2\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}\cdot\|\tilde{y}-\bar{y}\|_{2}\bigr).

Taking
\epsilon
small enough to ensure that
C\epsilon\leq 1/2
and applying Jensen’s inequality yields

\frac{1}{4}\bigl(\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}+\|\tilde{y}-\bar{y}\|_{2}\bigr)^{2}\leq C\epsilon\cdot\bigl(\|\tilde{x}-\bar{x}\|_{2}+\|\tilde{y}-\bar{y}\|_{2}\bigr),

from which the conclusion follows.
∎

Lemma
C.3
implies that, for each saddle-point update (
2b
),
(\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{v}_{k+1})
is a measurable function of the data
\bm{X}
. In turn, this implies that if we interleave first-order updates and saddle-point updates to obtain the collection of iterates
\bm{U}_{k}=[\bm{u}_{1}|\ldots|\bm{u}_{k}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
\bm{V}_{k}=[\bm{v}_{1}|\ldots|\bm{v}_{k}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
, there exists an almost surely unique disintegration (see, e.g.,
Chang and Pollard 1997
) of the joint distribution
\mathsf{Law}\bigl(\bm{X},(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{V}_{k})\bigr)
into conditional distributions
\mathsf{Law}(\bm{X}\,|\,\bm{U}_{k},\bm{V}_{k})
, which we determine in the next lemma. To this end, let
F\subseteq[k]
denote the indices for which a first-order update (
2a
) was taken and
S\subseteq[k]
with
S\cap F=\emptyset
denote the indices for which a saddle-point update (
2b
) was taken. Further, let
\tilde{F}=\{\ell\in F:\ell+1\in F\}
and define the pair of subspaces

\mathbb{U}_{k}:=\mathrm{span}\bigl(\{\bm{u}_{\ell}:\,\ell\in\tilde{F}\cup S\}\bigr)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathbb{V}_{k}:=\mathrm{span}\bigl(\{\bm{v}_{\ell}:\,\ell\in\tilde{F}\cup S\}\bigr).

Equipped with these definitions, we next have our main conditioning lemma.

Lemma C.4
.

Let
\bm{X}=(X_{ij})_{i\in[n],j\in[d]}
and suppose that
X_{ij}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathsf{N}(0,1)
. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and for
\ell\leq k
, let
(\bm{u}_{\ell},\bm{v}_{\ell})
be constructed from the updates (
1
)-(
2
). Writing
\bm{U}_{k}=[\bm{u}_{1}|\ldots|\bm{u}_{k}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
and
\bm{V}_{k}=[\bm{v}_{1}|\ldots|\bm{v}_{k}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
, let
\mathcal{F}_{k}=\sigma(\bm{U}_{k},\bm{V}_{k})
. Then, with
\widetilde{\bm{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
an independent copy of
\bm{X}
,

\bm{X}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k}\overset{d}{=}\bm{P}_{\mathbb{U}_{k}}\bm{X}\bm{P}_{\mathbb{V}_{k}}+\bm{P}_{\mathbb{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\bm{X}\bm{P}_{\mathbb{V}_{k}}+\bm{P}_{\mathbb{U}_{k}}\bm{X}\bm{P}_{\mathbb{V}_{k}}^{\perp}+\bm{P}_{\mathbb{U}_{k}}^{\perp}\widetilde{\bm{X}}\bm{P}_{\mathbb{V}_{k}}^{\perp}.

Proof.

Let
F,\tilde{F},S
be as defined before the statement of the lemma. For each
\ell\in S\cup\tilde{F}
, let
\bm{h}_{\ell}=\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\ell}
and
\bm{b}_{\ell}=\bm{X}\bm{v}_{\ell}
. Note that, by Lemma
5.3
, it holds that
\bm{X}\bm{v}_{\ell}=\nabla\phi_{\ell}^{u}(\bm{u}_{\ell})=\bm{b}_{\ell}
and
\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\ell}=-\nabla\phi_{\ell}^{v}(\bm{v}_{\ell})=\bm{h}_{\ell}
. Next, form
\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\lvert S\cup\tilde{F}\rvert}
and
\widetilde{\bm{V}}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times\lvert S\cup\tilde{F}\rvert}
by collecting the vectors
\{\bm{u}_{\ell}:\,\ell\in S\cup\tilde{F}\}
and
\{\bm{v}_{\ell}:\,\ell\in S\cup\tilde{F}\}
into matrices. We then claim that, for all
U\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k},V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k},H\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times\lvert S\cup\tilde{F}\rvert}
, and
B\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times\lvert S\cup\tilde{F}\rvert}
, the following events are equivalent.

\Bigl\{\bm{U}_{k}=U,\,\bm{V}_{k}=V,\bm{H}_{k}=H,\bm{B}_{k}=B\Bigr\}=\bigcup_{\ell\in S\cup\tilde{F}}\Bigl\{\bm{X}v_{\ell}=b_{\ell},\,-\bm{X}^{\top}u_{\ell}=h_{\ell}\Bigr\}.

Note that for first-order updates, this equivalence is by definition, and for saddle-point updates this follows from Lemma
C.3
. Thus,
\mathcal{F}_{k}
is equivalent to a set of linear constrains on
\bm{X}
. The desired claim then follows from standard properties of Gaussian matrices
[see, e.g.,
Montanari and Wu 2023
, Lemma 3.1]
.
∎

C.3
Sub-exponential concentration for pseudo-Lipschitz functions

Our last ancillary lemma is a concentration inequality for order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz functions of (normalized) Gaussian vectors. The proof is a straightforward modification of the Gaussian Lipschitz concentration inequality
[
Tao 2012
,
Wainwright 2019
, see, e.g.,]
and builds upon the Maurey–Pisier interpolation technique (see
Milman and Schechtman 1986
).

Lemma C.5
.

Suppose that
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is an order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz function with constant
L
. If
X\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_{d})
, then

\bigl\|f(X/\sqrt{d})-\mathbb{E}[f(X/\sqrt{d})]\bigr\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\frac{\pi}{2}\sqrt{\frac{5}{\ln(2)}}\cdot\frac{L}{\sqrt{d}}.

Proof.

First, note that since
f
is order-
2
pseudo-Lipschitz, it is locally Lipschitz so that by Rademacher’s theorem it is differentiable almost everywhere with respect to the Lebesgue measure. Hence, it suffices to prove the claim for functions
f
which are both differentiable as well as pseudo-Lipschitz.

Next, let
g:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be such that
g(x)=f(x/\sqrt{d})
. Fix
\lambda>0
, and apply
Wainwright 2019
to the convex function
t\mapsto e^{\lambda t}
to obtain the bound

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\exp\bigl\{\lambda\bigl(g(X)-\mathbb{E}[g(X)]\bigr)\bigr\}\Bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\exp\Bigl\{\frac{\lambda\pi}{2}\nabla g(X)^{\top}Y\Bigr\}\Bigr]=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\exp\Bigl\{\frac{\lambda^{2}\pi^{2}}{8}\|\nabla g(X)\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr\}\Bigr],

where
Y\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I_{d})
and is independent of
X
. From the pseudo-Lipschitz nature of
f
, we deduce that
\|\nabla g(X)\|_{2}\leq\frac{L}{\sqrt{d}}(1+2\|X/\sqrt{d}\|_{2})
. Consequently,

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\exp\bigl\{\lambda\bigl(g(X)-\mathbb{E}[g(X)]\bigr)\bigr\}\Bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\exp\Bigl\{\frac{\lambda^{2}L^{2}\pi^{2}}{4d}\Bigl(1+\frac{4}{d}\|X\|_{2}^{2}\Bigr)\Bigr\}\Bigr]\leq\exp\Bigl\{\frac{5\lambda^{2}L^{2}\pi^{2}}{4d}\Bigr\},

where the final inequality holds for all
\lambda<d/(L\pi\sqrt{2})
. We thus deduce that

\bigl\|g(X)-\mathbb{E}[g(X)]\bigr\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\frac{\pi}{2}\sqrt{\frac{5}{\ln(2)}}\cdot\frac{L}{\sqrt{d}},

which concludes the proof.
∎
